# Epics, stories and sprints

This file is the board. Only the lead changes a story's status or owner. Other agents report to the lead.

Current sprint: **Sprint 3** (started 2026-10-25). Snapshot: `../app-snapshots/sprint-3/`. Sprint 2 ended 2026-10-25 (snapshot `../app-snapshots/sprint-2/`); Sprint 1 ended 2026-10-25 (snapshot `../app-snapshots/sprint-1/`); Sprint 0 snapshot `../app-snapshots/sprint-0/`.
Lead rulings on spec questions: [spec/decisions.md](../spec/decisions.md).

## How a story moves

`todo` → `ready` → `building` → `built` → `checked` → `verified` → `attacked` (security stories) → `qa` (screen stories) → `done`

## Definition of Ready

A story can start when:
- its spec cards exist and a second Spec agent reviewed them;
- its acceptance checks are written;
- its files and crate are known, and no other active story owns those files;
- the stories it depends on are Done.

## Definition of Done

A story is Done when:
- code and tests are written;
- `scripts/check.sh` passes;
- a Checker reviewed it (two Checkers for crypto, file format, `unsafe` and secret handling);
- a Verify agent signed it off with proof;
- its parity rows in [security.md](security.md) are updated;
- screen stories passed Mac QA against [design/option-a/mockup.html](../design/option-a/mockup.html), with a parity report per [BUILD-AND-VERIFY.md](../design/option-a/BUILD-AND-VERIFY.md);
- the name guard passes;
- no Critical or High finding is open for it.

## Sprints

One sprint is one week. Agents work in parallel inside a sprint.

| Sprint | Focus | The owner sees at the end |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | E0 foundation; E1 cards for format, crypto and keys | `scripts/check.sh` runs; the name guard works; first spec cards |
| 1 | E1 the rest (all cards, parity matrix, settings inventory); E2 crypto | Crypto crate with every test vector passing |
| 2 | E3 reading version 4, keys, integrity, key files | A test prints entry titles from reference-made demo vaults |
| 3 | E3 writing, version 3, fuzzing; E4 model, editing, history, recycle bin | Our saved vaults open in the reference CLI |
| 4 | E4 search, generator, TOTP, health, merge, import and export, settings | Feature tests pass against spec cases |
| 5 | E5 shell, session, unlock, main window; E6 clipboard and lock | The app opens a demo vault on this Mac; Mac QA report with screenshots |
| 6 | E5 editor, generator, reports, new vault, settings, saving; E6 Touch ID, hardening, screen protection | The full app works with demo vaults |
| 7 | E12 command line tool; E9 SSH agent; E10 hardware keys | Command line parity table; SSH and hardware key demos |
| 8 | E8 Auto-Type; E7 bridge start | Auto-Type demo |
| 9 | E7 bridge, passkeys, our extension (decision D4) | Browser fill on demo sites |
| 10 | E13 security verification fleet | Parity matrix all verified; attack report |
| 11 | E14 release preparation; E11 if in scope | Signed build candidate; open decisions |

Each sprint ends with a short report to the owner: what works and how to try it, proof, findings, decisions needed.

---

## E0 Foundation

Goal: every check runs with one command, before any feature code exists.

**E0-S1 Toolchain and workspace.** Install the Rust toolchain. Pin it in `rust-toolchain.toml`. Create the workspace and the first crates from [architecture.md](architecture.md) (`app-crypto`, `app-model`, `app-format`, `app-core`, `app-platform`, `app-desktop`, `app-cli`), each with its `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` rule and the release profile.
- Checks: `cargo build --workspace` and `cargo test --workspace` pass; `cargo tree` shows approved crates only.
- Status: done (2026-10-24). Proof: cargo build/test/clippy -D warnings/fmt pass; 7 crates, forbid(unsafe_code) via workspace lints (app-platform: deny); release profile per rule 11; toolchain 1.99.1 pinned; 38 approved crates pinned exactly. Checker passed (2 rounds), Verify signed off.

**E0-S2 One check command.** `scripts/check.sh` runs gate G1, gate G2, `cargo deny check`, `cargo audit`, and a fuzz smoke run once fuzz targets exist.
- Checks: exits with an error on any failure; prints one summary line per gate; README explains how to run it.
- Status: done (2026-10-24). Proof: scripts/check.sh prints G1 static, G2 tests, deny, audit, fuzz smoke lines, overall PASS in about 6 s; injected fmt error, banned word, failing test and emptied guard word list each give exit 1. Checker passed, Verify signed off.

**E0-S3 Name guard.** `scripts/check-names.sh` scans every file name and every text file for the banned words. The words are stored encoded, so the script itself contains none.
- Checks: a temp file with a banned word makes it fail; removing the file makes it pass; it runs inside `check.sh`.
- Status: done (2026-10-24). Proof: names: PASS (75 files scanned, 12 vault files name-only); --self-test passes; Red team findings F-001 (High), F-002..F-005, F-009 (Medium), F-006..F-008 fixed and re-tested; Lows F-010..F-012 for a Sprint 1 tidy-up, rest backlog (security/findings.md). Checker passed, Verify signed off.

**E0-S4 Dependency policy.** `deny.toml` with a license allowlist (MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, ISC, Zlib, Unicode-3.0), advisories denied, crates.io as the only source, and bans on OpenSSL and native TLS.
- Checks: `cargo deny check` passes; adding `openssl-sys` makes it fail.
- Status: done (2026-10-24). Proof: cargo deny check passes; exact license allowlist; crates.io only; openssl, openssl-sys, native-tls banned; 68 crypto and key-encoding crates deny duplicates; adding openssl-sys in a scratch copy fails. Checker passed, Verify signed off.

**E0-S5 Test data kit.** `scripts/make-testdata.sh` uses `REF_CLI` to make demo vaults: password only, key file only, password plus key file, each cipher and KDF the CLI offers, attachments, history, recycle bin, custom fields, TOTP, and one vault with 10,000 entries. Each vault is exported to JSON as its expected content, with the creator field removed. `testdata/README.md` lists every file and its demo password.
- Checks: running the script again gives vaults whose exports match the stored JSON; no real data anywhere; the name guard passes.
- Status: done (2026-10-24). Proof: 12 demo vaults, 6 key files, 12 expected JSON files; --verify regenerates and matches; name guard passes. The reference CLI only makes AES-256 + AES-KDF (version 3.1, or 4.0/4.1 when content needs version 4; FMT-01.25); other variants per the FMT-01 interop matrix. Checker passed, Verify signed off.

**E0-S6 Board and evidence folders.** Create `spec/`, `security/` and `qa/` as [team.md](team.md) describes. Every agent brief uses the brief template.
- Checks: the first sprint report shows the board.
- Status: done (lead: spec/, security/parity/, qa/scripts/ created)

**E0-S7 Own every dependency** (owner 2026-10-29, LD-221): every outside package becomes App's own copy in `vendor/`; builds never download; small packages replaced by our own code; crypto copies unchanged except cut to what we use.
- Checks: `cargo build --offline --locked` and every test pass with no network and an empty cargo home; a check fails when any source outside the tree is reached; each copy has its license and version in the bill of materials; crypto copies match upstream byte-for-byte except removed files (diff proof); AT-B01 known answers pass on the copies.

## E2 Crypto (app-crypto)

Goal: every primitive we need, from approved crates, behind a small API, proven by test vectors.

**E2-S1 Secret types.** `SecretBytes`, `SecretString`.
- Checks: `Debug` prints `[hidden]`; `Clone` does not compile (compile-fail test); memory is zeroed after drop; equality is constant-time.
- Status: done (2026-10-25). Proof: Debug prints [hidden]; Clone fails to compile (compile_fail doctests with a control); memory zeroed after drop (test-only wipe hook, volatile stores kept in release LLVM IR); equality through subtle (call-count test; release timing equal for equal, differ-first, differ-last). Checker passed round 2 (13 of 15 mutants caught; the 2 survivors are documented limits). Verify signed off round 2; check.sh Overall PASS.

**E2-S2 Random source.**
- Checks: only `getrandom` is used; `below(n)` shows no bias over one million draws; an OS source failure becomes an error, never a weak value.
- Status: done (2026-10-25). Proof: a failing source gives an error, never a value (call-count tests; 4 bug variants each caught); exhaustive 2^32 uniformity test passes in release. Checker passed round 2, Verify signed off round 2; check.sh Overall PASS. argon2 default features off, so no second random path (AT-B04).

**E2-S3 Hashes and MACs.**
- Checks: FIPS 180-4 and RFC 4231 vectors pass; verify is constant-time.
- Status: done (2026-10-25). Proof: all 33 hash and HMAC known answers match python3 hashlib/hmac, 8 re-checked with openssl, RFC 4231, RFC 2202 and FIPS 180-4 texts; 1 GiB streaming at 3.2 MB peak; verify goes through one constant-time compare (counter test, source scan, AT-B05 timing |t| at most 1.09). Checker passed round 2, Verify signed off round 2.

**E2-S4 Ciphers.** AES-256-CBC, Twofish-CBC, ChaCha20.
- Checks: NIST SP 800-38A, RFC 8439 and Twofish reference vectors pass; bad padding and wrong key give the same error from the cipher API (spec/decisions.md LD-04); a 1 GB streaming test keeps memory flat.
- Status: done (2026-10-25). Proof: 118 known answers recomputed with outside tools (OpenSSL 4.0.3, libgcrypt 1.12.1, nettle 3.10.2, libsodium 1.0.22, python hashlib), 0 failed; LD-04/LD-37: 451,977 decrypt calls give only Ok or the one Decrypt error; 1 GiB streams at 7.8-8.5 MB peak; heap wipe proven with a spying allocator. Checker passed round 2 (every mutant dies), Verify signed off. Follow-up V-01 to V-03 (copies left by moves) fixed: state boxed, stack wiped after public calls; Verify re-scan: 0 secret bytes in freed heap and on the stack for every cipher, field stream and derive. Twofish runs at about 3 MB/s (crate limit, performance only).

**E2-S5 Key derivation.** Argon2d, Argon2id, AES-KDF, limits, benchmark.
- Checks: RFC 9106 vectors pass; AES-KDF matches card CRY-02; settings over the limits are refused before work starts; the benchmark lands at 1.0 to 1.2 times the target on this Mac (spec/decisions.md LD-19).
- Status: done (2026-10-25). Proof: RFC 9106 and CRY-02 vectors match OpenSSL's Argon2 KDF, AES-KDF matches libgcrypt; benchmark band 1.11x to 1.15x on a quiet Mac (3 runs, never below 1.0x; LD-19); 64 MiB Argon2 memory freed all zero. Checker passed round 2, Verify signed off. Rulings LD-38, LD-39, LD-40.

**E2-S6 Field streams.** ChaCha20 and Salsa20 keystreams for protected fields.
- Checks: standard vectors and card FMT-09 cases pass.
- Status: done (2026-10-25). Proof: ChaCha20 and Salsa20 field cases match OpenSSL, libgcrypt and libsodium; key and nonce derivation in app-crypto, cross-checked with sha2; state wiped on drop (compile-time check). Checker passed round 2, Verify signed off.

**E2-S7 Crypto review gate.** Two Checkers read the whole crate; the Red team runs AT-B01 to AT-B07.
- Checks: no open Critical or High finding.
- Status: done (2026-10-25). Proof: whole-crate Checker passed round 2 (stack probe clean in release and debug); Red team ran AT-B01 to AT-B07: all pass (AT-B06 with the thread CPU-time clock, LD-72); findings F-025 to F-033: every High and Medium fixed and re-tested, F-032 and F-033 Low accepted. Verify signed off: 142 tests and 7 compile_fail doctests pass; heap and stack scans 0 secret bytes for every public call; benchmark band 1.10x to 1.16x with a CPU-time clock; check.sh Overall PASS.

Run 1 (2026-10-25 20:39) stopped after 10 h 25 min when the Mac slept on low battery at 07:04; its 6 "timeout" files all fired at the 09:10 wake and each re-runs in 0 to 6 ms (false alarms, archived in fuzz/target/run1-2026-10-25/). Run 2: all 11 targets, fresh 24 hours on current code, 2026-10-26 13:40 to 2026-10-27 13:40, under `caffeinate -is` (logs fuzz/target/logs/). `references` stopped at 13:47 on its own 1-second wall-clock check (0.7 s in the sanitizer build under load, 0.28 s without; not a product fault): check widened to 5 s, the input kept as a seed, run restarted 15:24. `breach_lines` and `strength_estimate` (E4-S11) started 15:21. After the E4-S11 fix pass, `references`, `search_query`, `breach_lines` and `strength_estimate` restarted at 16:40 on the new code (earlier logs in fuzz/target/run2-superseded/). `references` then stopped on libFuzzer's 2,560 MB RSS limit in the sanitizer build while its live heap was 46 MB; without the sanitizer, 91,108 inputs plateau at 388 MB with no crash, so it is the sanitizer keeping freed memory. Restarted 16:52 with `ASAN_OPTIONS=allocator_release_to_os_interval_ms=1000`, `-rss_limit_mb=8192` and `-malloc_limit_mb=2560` (one allocation still capped at 2.5 GB). Stopped by the owner on 2026-10-27 04:20 (OD-05) because the runs filled the Mac's CPU: 10 targets ran 14 h 38 min, `search_query` and `breach_lines` 11 h 39 min, `references` 11 h 26 min, `strength_estimate` 10 h 8 min; 0 crashes, 0 timeouts, no crash files (`fuzz/target/logs/`). Accepted as AT-A10 proof for now; the full 24 hours per target (all 18 targets) moves to E13-S3. **Done** 2026-10-27: Verify signed off (AT-A10 stats confirmed, 0 crash files; AT-A11 3,655 mutated files, all agree except two differences the cards already intend, FMT-02.20 and FMT-09.15; proof security/parity/E3-S10-E3-S11-fuzz.md)

AT-I05 through the screen and the watch). Checker next. Lead 2026-10-28 (LD-211 (5)): Session::save moves into saving.rs, so this story's Checker and Verify also re-run the E5-S8 save tests (cry02_14, mod12_14, mod12_10, app07_16_t31, mod12_10_t18). Checker 1 2026-10-28: sent back (5). (1) A silent reload, Load or Discard (`take_disk_version`) sets the vault to the disk version and the change count to 0 whatever changed during its KDF: a change committed meanwhile is lost without a word (probe: "Typed meanwhile" gone, changes 0). Fix: read `s.changes` when the save lock is taken and merge when `merging || s.changes > before` (tried in a copy: 15 tests and the probe pass). (2) Lock Everything (⇧⌘L) and every automatic lock (`LockSignals` → `lockAll`) never save (APP-05.4, APP-02.15 step 8, APP-02.16 "saved as step 8 says"): with S-FILE-01 off and `save.on_lock` on, a per-vault autosave delay, the 150 ms window or after a failed save, a sleep or screen lock drops the changes (probe: the change is not in the file after `lockAll`). Fix: `lockAll` saves each changed unlocked vault first (the loop of `mayQuit(asks: false)`); the menu's Lock Everything asks when that fails. (3) Deadlock: `save` and the new reload/merge hold the save lock and then take the session lock, while `VaultSettings::view` and `change_key` hold the session lock and take the save lock (`EditState::key_parts`, `set_key`): opening Vault Settings during an auto-save or a reload hangs App (2 time-boxed probes, no end in 20 s). Fix: one lock order (save lock first, LD-199 (4)): no Saver lock inside `with_edit` (key-part facts as plain `EditState` fields; `set_key` after `with_edit` returns); touches E5-S8's vault_settings.rs, lead to route; add the time-boxed test. (4) S-FILE-01 is read only when the vault opens (`MainWindowModel` line 92): turning it off in Settings still saves each change (APP-05.3 T06; probe). Fix: read the flag live in `scheduleSave` (keep the test override). (5) Removing `sv.watched = seen` in `check_disk` fails no test (the question then repeats every 30 s after Cancel). Fix: assert that a second `check_disk` after an `Ask` answered Later gives `None`. Proof: app-ffi + app-core 43 binaries, 913 passed, 0 failed; the 15 saving tests and the E5-S8 save tests pass; the 6 ParitySavingTests and 3 snapshot cases pass; 13 of 14 Rust and 5 of 5 Swift mutations fail a test (copy only; shared files `shasum -c` OK); clippy, fmt, names clean; swift format and SwiftLint clean on E5-S10 files. Fixed 2026-10-28 (Builder): (1) `take_disk_version` gets the change count its decision saw (`check_disk`, Reload) and merges when it rose during the KDF, so a change typed during a silent reload, Load or Discard is kept (test `at_i05_a_change_made_during_a_silent_reload_is_kept`); (2) `lockAll(asks:)` saves each changed unlocked vault first (`saveAll`, shared with `mayQuit`); the Lock Everything menu items and the menu bar icon ask when that save fails, automatic locks never ask (Swift test "APP-02.15 step 8, APP-02.16: Lock Everything and automatic locks save first; a failed save asks on the menu"); (3) one lock order, save lock before session lock: the key parts' facts are plain `EditState` fields (`key_parts` takes no lock), `change_key` takes the save lock before `with_edit` and holds it to `set_key` (E5-S8's vault_settings.rs, 2 small hunks) (time-boxed test `ld_199_4_vault_settings_during_a_reload_or_a_save_never_hangs`); (4) S-FILE-01 is read live in `scheduleSave` and its timer (a test override stays) (Swift test "APP-05.3 T06: S-FILE-01 turned off while the vault is open stops the save after each change; on, it saves"); (5) `app_05_26` asserts that a second `check_disk` after Later gives `None`. Each fix undone in a private copy fails its test (6 of 6). Checker next.; Checker 1 re-check 2026-10-28: passed (fixes (1) to (5) hold; app-ffi + app-core 43 binaries, 915 passed, 0 failed; Swift 207 tests in 33 suites passed; 6 of 6 fix mutations fail their test, copy only; clippy, fmt, swift format, SwiftLint, names clean; testdata unchanged). Smallest fix: one LD row with the proven-app and Apple sources, plus the APP-05.24 and architecture wording. (2) An automatic lock or quit signal drops a changed vault's unsaved changes when its save fails or meets a changed file, with no recovery copy (APP-05.4, APP-05.28 T60, APP-02.16, LD-50; probe V-C T60: change gone, vault locked). (1) ⌘W with no vault open does nothing: the disabled Close Vault (⌘W) comes before Close Window (⌘W) and takes the key (probe: `NSHostingMenu(FileMenuItems)` `performKeyEquivalent` ⌘W returns handled and Close Window never runs; with Close Vault's key dropped while no vault is shown it runs). Fix: `.keyboardShortcut(window.shown == nil ? nil : KeyboardShortcut("w"))` on Close Vault, and a test that sends ⌘W through the menu with no vault. (2) ⌘W in the Settings, Vault Settings or New Vault window closes the shown vault instead of that window (Close Vault ignores the key window, unlike Close Window and Minimize). Fix: Close Vault closes the key window when it is not the main window, through a key-window seam in `WindowRules`, with a test. (3) The AT-K06 walk accepts a Phosphor private-use glyph as a name: with `IconButton`'s label removed it still passes (19 of 19). Fix: a name needs a non-space character outside U+E000 to U+F8FF; with that the walk passes on the built code and fails the mutant on 11 screens. (4) APP-09.21 (start minimized: hide to the icon, else into the Dock) has no Swift test. Fix: move it into `WindowRules` and test both branches. (5) A hidden launch (`HiddenLaunch`) with S-UI-10 on adds a real status item (`SystemStatusSlot`) to the owner's menu bar. Fix: no status item when `launchHidden()`, as `AuxWindows.present` does. ## E6 Platform security (app-platform, macos/)

**E6-S1 Clipboard** (SC-01 to SC-03): Rust `app-platform/clipboard.rs`, shared with `app-cli` (SC-07). Checks: AT-D01 to AT-D03 pass. AT-D01 with a real clipboard history app and AT-D02, AT-D03 on the general pasteboard wait for Verify. Checker 2026-10-28 (Swift side): sent back (3). Checker re-check 2026-10-28 (Swift side): sent back (1). Checker re-check 2026-10-28 (Swift side): passed. Verify 2026-10-28: signed (on private pasteboards, read by another process: one item, text plus both markers with 0 bytes; cleared at 323 ms for a 300 ms delay; another process's newer write left alone; a new copy restarts the countdown; the session's 10 s copy is gone at 10.6 s; lock, lock-all, close, a lock of another vault and quit clear App's copy and keep newer content; `Copied` holds label, seconds and empty only; no value in 450 strings of Swift state or in the logs; codes equal app-core and RFC 6238; fingerprints equal `ssh-keygen -lf`; 71 hostile calls refused, nothing written; the shown unlock password has no way out (probe and ShownPasswordFieldTests); empty values copy nothing; `states: 13/13 PASS`, states-copied 1.58 % / 1.62 %; cargo tests 0 failed; Swift 47 tests in 11 suites passed; general pasteboard change count 10124 before and after). **E6-S4 Process hardening** (SR-05). Checks: AT-C03 passes; `codesign -dv` shows the hardened runtime flag. Status: **Done** 2026-10-28 (Verify signed; proof in [E6-S4-hardening.md](../security/parity/E6-S4-hardening.md): release builds core limit 0/0 and ptrace attach EBUSY plus SIGSEGV (control EPERM) in the app-platform tests under `--release`, in a new Release `App.app` run hidden at its password prompt and in the release `app` tool; debug builds skip both and carry `get-task-allow`; Release bundle `flags=0x10002(adhoc,runtime)`, no entitlements, `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES` ignored; the nine self-test values match libgcrypt, three also match RFC 7539, RFC 4231 and RFC 9106 byte for byte; lldb checks and the forced crash wait for Mac QA (rule 10, LD-174); APP-11.10-b needs a debug fault hook in app-ffi, APP-11.10-c shown only with the debug tool hook). Built 2026-10-28 (app-platform `hardening.rs` and `macos/process.rs`: release builds set the core limit to 0, then `ptrace(PT_DENY_ATTACH)`, a failed step writes M11-01 and start goes on, debug builds skip both; app-crypto `selftest.rs`: the nine APP-11.10 known answers, a wrong one gives M11-02 through the APP-09 refuse path, exit 1; 8 new tests, among them a child-process ptrace attach that gets EBUSY plus SIGSEGV after deny-attach (control without it: EPERM) and the same through `harden()` under `cargo test --release`; the existing release bundles show `flags=0x10002(adhoc,runtime)`, no entitlements); app-level AT-C03 on a new release bundle waits for Verify; unsafe, Checker 1: `macos/process.rs:16` setrlimit sound, `:33` ptrace(PT_DENY_ATTACH) sound, test-only `:57` getrlimit, `:85` setrlimit, `:116` signal (the handler only stores an atomic) and `:119` ptrace(PT_ATTACHEXC) sound; the nine self-test values confirmed with python, OpenSSL 4.0.3, nettle and libgcrypt; second Checker for the unsafe next; Checker 1 2026-10-28: passed; unsafe, second Checker: `macos/process.rs:16`, `:33`, `:57`, `:85`, `:116` and `:119` sound (types and values match `ptrace(2)`, `setrlimit(2)`, `signal(3)` and libc 0.2.191; errno read right after each call; the handler only stores a lock-free atomic; all test code under `#[cfg(test)]`, and a release `app` imports no `getrlimit`), `macos/terminal.rs:216` call sound (SIG_DFL, empty mask, no flags), and `put_back`'s doc (`:144`) and SAFETY note (`:148`), reworded, now cover all four callers (`:58`, `:102`, `:137` put back what the system returned; `:216` SIG_DFL), `cargo test -p app-platform` 15 passed in debug and release, app09_16 passes in the foreground and in the background (where SIGINT and SIGQUIT start ignored), debug and release; unsafe second Checker 2026-10-28: signed
**E6-S5 Screen protection and secure input** (SC-05, SR-06; APP-09 flag F07, LD-153): Swift `sharingType` on every window and secure input. Checks: AT-D04 and AT-D05 pass. **E6-S7 Network client for opt-in features** (SN-01 to SN-04). Checks: network capture on first start shows no traffic; a bad certificate is refused. Proof in [E6-S7-network-client.md](../security/parity/E6-S7-network-client.md): a hidden debug App and the release `app` each watched 20 s from their first instruction with `nettop` and `lsof` per process, 0 internet sockets (control seen); no ureq or rustls in `cargo tree` or `nm`; the product client refused self-signed, expired, not yet valid, wrong host, untrusted chain and untrusted good leaves, the test-anchor path took the good leaf and chain; TLS 1.0 and 1.1 servers refused, 1.2 and 1.3 ok, SNI sent; redirects, cookies and proxy variables ignored; size and time limits held, handshake budget included; AT-J03 conditions held; app-platform with `net` 33 and 1 passed; open: a not-yet-valid certificate reads "it has expired" (macOS gives -67818 for both). Built 2026-10-28 (LD-194: app-platform `net.rs`, feature `net`, off by default and on in no product build, so app-ffi and app-cli link no network code (SN-05); `Client` with connect, per-read and total time limits (15, 30, 30 s by default, FEA-06.8), a body limit per call, User-Agent `App`, no cookies, no proxy, no compression, no redirect followed (a 3xx is an error), https only, http only through `get_icon` (SN-03), no user name or password in a URL (LD-64); TLS 1.2 and 1.3 by rustls on ring, under ureq through App's own small connector, certificates checked by macOS trust evaluation through rustls-platform-verifier; 9 tests against servers on 127.0.0.1 only, certificates made per run by rcgen, the test root trusted in memory for one client, never a keychain: good certificate works, self-signed, expired, wrong host and the product client without the test root refused with plain-language reasons, connect, read and total timeouts fire, the TLS handshake gets only what is left of the connect and total limits after a 400 ms TCP connect (Checker 1 item 1), body over the limit refused and exactly the limit read, no cookie kept or sent, redirects to the same or another host not followed, plain http refused outside icons, SN-01 a new client opens no file or socket and the module has no global or thread; `cargo deny check` ok (deny.toml sees `app-platform/net`; named getrandom 0.2.17 skip for ring); open: AT-J03 for the new crates, the network capture on first start (needs the Mac app and a capture tool), the callers (breach button E5-S7, icon download, update check E14)); AT-J03 signed with conditions (LD-197); to fix: (1) `net.rs:271` the TLS handshake restarts the connect budget, so connect and total limits overshoot (probe: 500 ms connect gave up at 909 ms, 600 ms total at 1,012 ms; worst case 45 s, not FEA-06.8's 30 s), (2) `scripts/check.sh:92` and `:103` never build, lint or test `net.rs` (no `--features net`), (3) `net.rs:50` says the connect limit covers the name lookup, which only `total` bounds; ## E7 Browser bridge and passkeys (app-browser, extension/)

D4 decided 2026-10-31 (LD-272): every major browser, Chrome and Safari first.
Goal: App's own extension fills from App in every major browser, with no outside code and no fill without a user action. Looks: the "Browser extension" section of [design/option-a/mockup.html](../design/option-a/mockup.html) (15 frames, LD-162). Design and sources: [spec/research/browser-and-web.md](../spec/research/browser-and-web.md) B1 to B12 (the lead's LD rows); parts: [architecture.md](architecture.md), "Browser extension and web vault (E7, E20)". Store accounts, fees and publishing are owner steps (LD-272 (4)); everything is built and tested on this Mac first.
Order: E7 runs beside the sync epics within the 4-agent limit, and the sync, family and teams order of LD-262 (2) stays (LD-272 (5)). E7-S7b starts at once (it owns only `extension/test/`). E7-S7a waits for E16-S1 (both edit spec/README.md's list) and for the lead's LD row on B3 (written: LD-274). E7-S1 waits for E7-S7a (its INT-01 channel edit) and for E16-S2 (`x25519-dalek` and the direct `aes-gcm`); under B3 (LD-274 (1)) the `crypto_box` re-pin of LD-203 is dropped, because `crypto_box` has no stable release on App's crypto generation (research section 8). E7-S2 and E7-S3 wait for E7-S1. E7-S4 waits for E7-S2 and E7-S3. E7-S5 waits for E7-S2, E7-S3 and the lead's ruling on R04-1 (LD-215). E7-S6 waits for E7-S1 and the E0-S7 follow-up (it adds the `app-bridge` crate to the Cargo files and a step to scripts/build-core.sh and scripts/repro-build.sh). Chrome first: E7-S7c waits for E7-S7a, E7-S7b, E7-S1 and E7-S6; E7-S7d waits for E7-S7c. Safari second: E7-S7e waits for E7-S7d. Then the screens, each checked in Chrome and in WebKit: E7-S7f waits for E7-S7e, E7-S2 and E7-S3; E7-S7g waits for E7-S7f and E7-S4; E7-S7h waits for E7-S7g; E7-S7i waits for E7-S7h and E7-S5. E7-S7k (frames only) waits for E7-S7a and may run beside the others. E7-S7j (Firefox) and E7-S7l (the other Chromium browsers) wait for E7-S7i and run one at a time; E7-S7l also waits for E7-S7k. E7-S7m waits for E7-S7j and E7-S7l. E7-S7n waits for E7-S7m and the owner's go.
Shared files (team.md: never two active stories on one file): `extension/build.py` and `extension/manifest.json.in` (E7-S7c writes them with every page and permission INT-11 names; E7-S7e, E7-S7j and E7-S7l each add their browser's part); `extension/background.js` (E7-S7c, then E7-S7d and E7-S7f to E7-S7i each add one module and its line in the routing table); `macos/App.xcodeproj` and `macos/App.entitlements` (E7-S6 and E7-S7e) and App's settings files `macos/App/AppSettingsParts.swift` and `macos/App/AppSettingsMorePages.swift` (E7-S7l): one at a time, and never beside another macos/ story that edits them (E15-S3, E16-S9, E17-S4, E18-S7); `design/option-a/proposals.html` (E7-S7k, E11-S4, E16-S8, E20-S6); `spec/README.md` (E7-S7a, E16-S1, E18-S1, E20-S1); `security/findings.md` (E7-S7m and any other Red team run). The order above already runs each group one at a time. Earlier mentions of "E7-S7" (INT-01, INT-02, INT-04) now mean E7-S7a to E7-S7n; INT-01.9's store ids are fixed in E7-S7n.

**E7-S1 Protocol and encrypted channel** with our own names and host id (INT-01). The channel is X25519, HKDF-SHA256 and AES-256-GCM in app-crypto `bridge.rs` with E16-S2's crates, and WebCrypto in the extension (LD-274 (1)); first remove the `crypto_box` pin from Cargo.toml and its copies from vendor/ (the re-pin of LD-203 is dropped). Checks (Safari): the channel tests also pass over the Safari socket route, where each message may arrive on a new connection and the session is found by `sid`; a Safari session ends after 15 minutes without a request (INT-01.14). LD-307 amends this story (it runs beside E16-S3: no outside crate, exact edits to Cargo.toml and `Cargo.lock`, no app-ffi or macos/ edits). Status: Done 2026-11-01 (Verify signed; Builder, Checker 1 rounds 1 to 4, Checker 2; lead rounds LD-307, LD-308, LD-312): app-crypto `bridge.rs` and the new crate `app-browser` (hello, sid sessions, the bridge and Safari socket routes, INT-01.14's 15-minute end); CV-2 to CV-9 equal in app-crypto, WebCrypto in headless Chrome and Apple CryptoKit; the INT-01.25 size limit (LD-312); fuzz target `browser_message`, T65 10 minutes at frames up to 1 MiB, clean; workspace 126 binaries, 2,877 passed, 0 failed; F-188 to F-192, F-198, F-199, F-203, F-204, F-211 and F-215 fixed; `crypto_box` and `crypto_secretbox` gone from the Cargo files, deny.toml and vendor/.

**E7-S7a Spec card for the extension (INT-11) and the channel edit.** A Spec agent writes `spec/integrations/INT-11-browser-extension.md` in the card template, added to spec/README.md's list: the parts and what each may hold, the manifest and permissions per browser (B2), the identification key as a non-extractable WebCrypto key (B3), the port and reconnect (B4), sender checks and fill on a user action, with the limits of "Fill when a page loads" (B9), the in-page frame rules and the clickjacking checks (B10), the 15 frames' states with message ids, and each browser's minimum version from a check. Once the lead's LD row takes B3, it edits INT-01's channel rules (INT-01.20, INT-01.21, INT-01.23, INT-01.26, INT-01.28, D-INT01-2 and their test cases) to the WebCrypto channel, with known-answer vectors (RFC 7748, RFC 5869, published AES-GCM vectors, and channel vectors of its own). It proposes the AT-F09 row and the Chromium and Arc rows of INT-01.8 for the lead.
- Checks: every rule has an ID and at least one test case; INT-01's request table, errors, relay and identity rules keep their results; a second Spec agent reviewed the card and each edit; every choice links spec/research/browser-and-web.md or a standard; "How this was learned" names public sources only; the name guard passes.
- Status: **Done** 2026-11-01 (Verify signed, round 4; Spec reviews 1 to 6; lead rounds LD-295, LD-298, LD-299): spec/integrations/INT-11-browser-extension.md reviewed, 44 rules and 89 tests, the 15 frames' states with message ids, each browser's minimum version from BCD and WebKit's release notes; INT-01's channel rules rewritten to X25519, HKDF-SHA256 and AES-256-GCM with published vectors recomputed (RFC 7748, RFC 5869, NIST CAVP) and App's channel vectors left for E7-S1; INT-01.8's Chromium and Arc rows and INT-01.30's `disconnect` row added; AT-F09, SI-01 and spec/README.md updated; name guard passes.

**E7-S7b Test driver for headless Chrome.** `extension/test/driver.py` (Python standard library) starts the installed Chrome with `--headless=new`, `--remote-debugging-pipe`, `--enable-unsafe-extension-debugging`, a throwaway `--user-data-dir` and a test HOME under /tmp, loads an unpacked extension with `Extensions.loadUnpacked`, opens pages, runs scripts, takes screenshots, and stops Chrome by its PID (B11). It also sets up the test sites every later story uses, never an IP host (WebAuthn and INT-04.10 refuse IP hosts, and INT-02.19 takes two ports of one IP as one site): the names `site-a.test`, `site-b.test` and `sub.site-a.test` mapped to 127.0.0.1 with `--host-resolver-rules` (`.test` is reserved, RFC 6761); an https demo server (Python standard library) with a test certificate made for the run and trusted only by the throwaway profile through `--ignore-certificate-errors-spki-list`; and a two-file echo host for native messaging.
- Checks: a two-file demo extension in `extension/test/` loads and its id comes back; its service worker answers a message; a screenshot of its page is written; an https demo page on `site-a.test` loads with no certificate error, and `navigator.credentials.create` works on it with a WebAuthn virtual authenticator; a `site-b.test` frame in a `site-a.test` page has the other origin; the echo host answers `connectNative` when its manifest sits in the folder App writes under the test HOME (INT-01.8), and if Chrome with the throwaway profile reads another folder (community reports name `<user-data-dir>/NativeMessagingHosts`), the story stops and reports; no window shows and focus never moves (rule 10); Chrome's own profile and the owner's Library are untouched (modification times before and after); if `Extensions.loadUnpacked` is refused, the story stops and reports, and Chrome for Testing waits for the owner's yes (rule 7).
- Status: Done 2026-11-01 (Verify signed; Builder, Checker 1 in 4 rounds, Checker 2 in 3 rounds, LD-276, LD-278, LD-285, LD-286): the headless Chrome driver runs only the installed Chrome with a throwaway profile under a /tmp test HOME, --use-mock-keychain, resolver rules to 127.0.0.1 only, no updater, no DNS error-page probe, no code-sign clone and its temp files in the run's folder; a visibility guard and the keychain check by process; the demo extension loads and the native host answers only from the INT-01.8 folder; 37 tests OK 3 times; F-147 to F-151 and F-153 re-tested and fixed; sockets to 127.0.0.1 only, 0 DNS lookups, no keychain commit, no window or focus change, the owner's files unchanged

## E8 Auto-Type (app-autotype)

**E8-S1 Sequence parser and placeholders** (INT-05); fuzz target; AT-G03 passes. Status: **Done** 2026-10-28 (Verify signed; proof in [E8-S1-autotype-parser.md](../security/parity/E8-S1-autotype-parser.md): `cargo test -p app-autotype` 52 passed, every INT-05 case of E8-S1 mapped to a test (T01 to T41, the planner halves of T98 and T108, T99, R05-2/3/4/7, LD-184 (1) to (3)); own probe crate over the public API, 214 checks written from the card, 213 match (every key name and code, modifiers, counts, waits and pauses, `{CLEARFIELD}`, `{PICKCHARS}`, `{MODE=}`, ignored commands, escapes, control characters in sequence and values, MOD-08 placeholders and references inside the vault); AT-G03: 32 levels read and 33 give MI05-25 (1,000,000 levels in 87 ms), counts of 100,000 digits MI05-12 in 4 ms, 10,000 keys pass and 10,001 give MI05-21, 60,000 ms pass and 60,001 give MI05-22, 256 picked characters pass and 257 give MI05-18, 10 self-reference and growth shapes give MI05-27 in under 1 ms, a 64 MiB sequence at most 387 MB peak by `/usr/bin/time -l` and at most 3.2 s; R05-4 MI05-26 for every written form of the shortcut, other modifiers pass; `{TOTP}` late when written, at plan time through a field or reference, MI05-16 on invalid settings on every path; plan `Debug` shows `[hidden]` and 18 failing sequences hold no value; T40 fuzz 601 s, 144,097 runs, no crash, no timeout, slowest of the 2,116 corpus inputs 32 ms in release, `tests/data` 18.4 ms and 10.8 ms in release. Open, not blocking: G1 escaped braces inside a placeholder are not defined by INT-05.13 (`{S:a{}}b}` types `b}`), a card line for Spec; actions that type nothing (`{DELAY 0}`, `{DELAY=n}`) are not counted, so 64 MiB of them is 7.46 million actions, 324 MB; for E8-S3 the layout table needs modifier forms for key-code mode). Built 2026-10-28: `crates/app-autotype` (the reader with its own stack: escapes, brace count, tokens, nesting at most 32 (R05-2); the planner: every key name, modifiers, repeats, `{DELAY}`, `{DELAY=}`, `{CLEARFIELD}` as ⌘A then Delete, `{VKEY}` and the other ignored commands, `{MODE=}`, `{PICKCHARS}` up to 256 characters, `{TOTP}` marked to compute when typed, control characters per INT-05.14 and R05-3, App's own shortcut refused (R05-4), placeholders through app-core MOD-08 with one work meter per plan, a bound hit shown as MI05-27 (R05-7, no app-core change needed); at most 10,000 keys and 60 s of waits; MI05-11 to MI05-27 as data; key combinations through a layout table). Sequences and inheritance reuse app-core `entry_sequence` (MOD-02). Checker 1 fixes (LD-184) 2026-10-28: the reader keeps one 4-byte record per `{` and walks plain text in place (10 MB sequence: 518 to 610 MB peak before, 14 to 52 MB after), `{F01}` and `{NUMPAD00}` are not keys, a `{TOTP}` reached through a field fails with MI05-16 on invalid settings. 52 tests named by T-number (T01 to T39, T41, T40 timing, the planner half of T98, T99, T108, R05-2/3/4/7, LD-184 (1) to (3)). Fuzz target `autotype_sequence` with 12 seeds (deep nesting included): 60 s, 79,203 runs, no crash; worst known input 18 ms in release. Checker next. (1) The reader keeps a 48-byte item for every character of the sequence, in a Vec grown by doubling, so a plan or editor check takes about 100 bytes of memory per sequence byte: 2 MB took 229 MB, 10 MB took 1.0 GB, and a sequence at the vault's 64 MiB text limit would take about 6 GB (AT-G03); fix: keep items only for braces and escapes and walk the other characters in place, or shrink the item and reserve once, with a 10 MB test. (2) `{F01}` to `{F09}` and `{NUMPAD00}` to `{NUMPAD09}` press keys, but INT-05.6 names only `F1` to `F16` and `NUMPAD0` to `NUMPAD9`, so they should be placeholders and fail with MI05-19; fix: refuse a leading zero in `named`, and add them to a test. Notes, no change asked: a `{TOTP}` inside a field (`{S:Code}`) is resolved when the plan is made, not when typed, and invalid settings there type nothing; for E8-S2, the editor check needs the vault's own entry, or `{REF:…}` gives MI05-19 (MOD-08.29). Grammar, the other bounds, R05-2/3/4/7, D-INT05-8, the secret types and Debug output all hold; 12 T-cases sampled match the card. Checker 1 2026-10-28: sent back (2). Re-check: LD-184 (1) to (3) hold; each test fails with its fix undone (reader records not reserved: `ld184_1` fails; leading zeros allowed: `t11_leading_zeros_are_not_keys` fails; `{TOTP}` through MOD-08's plain code: `ld184_3_totp_through_a_field` fails), files restored and equal by `cmp`; peak memory in release, input included: 10 MB plain 13 MB, 64 MiB plain 70 MB, 64 MiB of tokens 123 MB, 64 MiB nested 315 MB, 64 MiB of `{DELAY 0}` 340 MB (was about 100 bytes per byte); cargo test -p app-autotype 52 passed, clippy and fmt clean; fuzz 60 s, 45,705 runs, no crash, no slow unit. Checker 1 re-check 2026-10-28: passed
**E8-S2 Window matching, global shortcut, confirm-before-typing option;** zero waits count toward the action limit (LD-186); AT-G01 and AT-G02 pass. Status: **Done** 2026-10-29 (Verify signed; proof in [E8-S2-autotype-matching.md](../security/parity/E8-S2-autotype-matching.md): round 3: the three round-2 findings fixed, each fix's story test fails with it undone (S0 `back = nil` out: INT-05.29 test; R15, R15b, R15c: `s_at_02_…`); whole-tree SwiftLint 0.65.1 `--strict` exit 0 (0 violations in 115 files); Rust 1,034 tree tests pass (26 ignored), Swift full run 279 tests with every E8-S2 test passing (AutoTypeTests 14 of 14; the only failures are other stories' tests); 18 Rust and 9 Swift own probes pass; AT-G01 and AT-G02 at the model level; LD-186 zero waits counted, 2 of 2 kinds; frame menubar-auto-type-picker PASS L 0.63 %, D 1.41 %; 30 of 33 mutants fail a story test (R17 only a probe; R18 and S5 none, notes N8 and N9); proof is for the 21:28 snapshot, E8-S3 now edits these files (N10); real hotkey, real windows and typing go to E8-S3 and Mac QA). History: Rust part built 2026-10-28 (`crates/app-autotype/src/matching.rs`: candidates in one tree walk (MOD-02.14 to MOD-02.16, bin left out, checked against app-core's functions); window rules: wildcards with only `*` special, `//…//` patterns through `app_core::pattern` with search's bounds (LD-113, LD-116, LD-120, LD-122, LD-123), case ignored by Unicode simple folding (the tables `(?i)` uses); title and URL matching (INT-05.21, INT-05.22; host in ASCII and Unicode form, R05-5); INT-05.23's order and repeats; R05-1; the picker model: `Picker::open`, `single` (INT-05.25 step 7), `rows` (FEA-01 search, INT-05.26 sort), `Memory` for S-AT-08 on a passed-in clock (INT-05.28, INT-05.31), `type_login_enabled`, `Settings` defaults; rows hold no secret (LD-191 R02-2); a pattern that reads a protected value matches nothing (LD-114); bounds: titles over 8,192 bytes are not matched, entry texts and row sequences over 65,536 bytes never match, one 150M-unit meter per call pays every resolution, compile, regex test, scan, fold and row, and a limited run always opens the picker; zero waits and pauses count toward the 10,000-action limit (LD-186 (2)). `cargo test -p app-autotype` 88 passed (33 new in `tests/int05_match.rs`: T42 to T59, T62 to T65, T70 to T73 and T87 at the model level, R05-1, R05-5, LD-114, LD-184 (4), LD-186 (2), R02-2, 6 hostile cases), clippy and fmt clean; three mutations (bin skip, LD-114 check, wait count) each fail tests. Release timings: 8 catastrophic-looking patterns on an 8,192-byte title 3 ms, a 1 MB title 1 µs, 1 MB entry texts 0.5 ms, 2,000 distinct patterns 3 ms (limited), 5,000 entries of growing texts 89 ms (limited), 10,000 entries 27 ms. New fuzz target `autotype_match` with two oracles against the pattern engine (wildcard and title match): 60 s, 46,422 runs, no crash, slowest unit under 1 s; `autotype_sequence` updated for LD-186 (2): 30 s, 36,918 runs, no crash). Checker 1 sent it back (LD-196); fixed 2026-10-28: rows hide parts that resolve from one-time-password and passkey fields (`ld196_1_…` fails without it); a limited run never types directly (`ld196_2_…` fails without it); a wildcard's last part must end the title (`glob_cases`, `ld196_3_…`); the Unicode host form is decoded into a wiping buffer in `app_core::url::host_unicode` (`host_unicode_decodes_every_xn_label`, `r05_5_…`); sort ignores case before the user name, case last (`app_core::edit::natural_cmp_folded`, `ld196_sort_…`); `actions` tells Swift whether Type Login, Username Only and Password Only are enabled (`ld196_actions_…`); MI05-21 counts keys, waits and pauses. Each test fails with its fix undone, files restored and equal by `cmp`; the Checker's probes p1 to p12 pass. `cargo test -p app-autotype -p app-core` 798 passed, clippy and fmt clean; fuzz `autotype_match` 60 s, 54,899 runs, no crash. Swift picker and shortcut next. Checker 1 findings: (1) a title or user name `{S:…}` of an unprotected one-time password or passkey field shows its value in a picker row (INT-05.45, LD-170 (4)); (2) no test fails when a limited run types directly; (3) no test fails when a wildcard's last part need not end the title; (4) the Unicode host decoder keeps unwiped copies of a protected URL's host. ## E9 SSH agent (app-sshagent)

**E9-S1 Agent client:** add and remove keys with lifetime and confirm limits (INT-06); AT-H01 and AT-H02 pass. Re-pin `ssh-key` first: the E0 pin is a release candidate. Status: **Done** 2026-10-28 (Verify signed; proof in [E9-S1-ssh-agent-client.md](../security/parity/E9-S1-ssh-agent-client.md): `cargo test -p app-sshagent` 32 passed, app-crypto `ssh` 3, the `pipe.rs` test in debug and `--release`; an own driver over the crate's API against throwaway OpenSSH 10.3 agents (cleared environment, stopped by PID): lifetime 2 s listed at 1.01 s and gone at 3.01 s by `ssh-add -l`, lifetime 0 and 1,000,000,000 MI06-17 with nothing sent, remove 12 ms with `ssh-add -l` (AT-H01), confirm refused without askpass and asked on each of 3 uses with a scripted askpass (AT-H02), remove absent, remove all with another program's key, list equal to `ssh-add -l`; 14 keys (Ed25519, ECDSA P-256/384/521, RSA 2048/3072/4096, plain and protected) read, unlock and list with fingerprints equal to `ssh-keygen -lf`; DSA MI06-12, 6 older forms MI06-11; a real 1,001-round key refused in under 1 ms, 1,000 accepted; 20 hostile fake-agent cases times 4 requests plus 1,200 random replies all typed, no panic, slowest 2.02 s; fuzz `ssh_key_read` 120 s, 9,116,923 runs, no crash. Open: T26's other-user socket needs the CI root fixture; V-INT06-3 (the system agent) waits for the owner's go; AT-H01's lock path and AT-H02 under (b) are E9-S2). Built 2026-10-28: `crates/app-sshagent` (add 17 or 25 with lifetime, confirm and the `sk-` provider limit; remove 18; remove all as one message 19; list 11/12; socket owner and peer checks; 2 s and 256 KiB bounds; one request at a time; MI06-01 to MI06-36 as data) and app-crypto `ssh.rs` (keys through `ssh-key`: DSA refused, older forms get the convert message, the 1,000-round cap checked first). 32 tests in app-sshagent plus 3 in app-crypto, against throwaway agents in /tmp and fake agents; AT-H01 (remove within 1 s), AT-H02 (T31 lifetime, T32 confirm). `ssh-key` stays `=0.7.0-rc.11`: no stable 0.7 exists, 0.6.7 is the older generation. AT-J03 at the E9-S1 Checker; Checker next. `macos/pipe.rs` unsafe, second Checker 2026-10-28: `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)` sound, note true, SIG_ERR and errno handled as signal(3) says; called first in `harden()`, before any socket; ignoring it process-wide is what Rust's standard library does before every Rust program's main (it sets SIGPIPE back to default in children it starts), as do Node.js and Chromium; no Swift child processes. 2 open items (a test that could not fail; LD-179 without sources) fixed 2026-10-28; re-check: the test's child sets SIGPIPE to SIG_DFL first; without the call it dies by signal 13 (status 141), with it exit 0; LD-179 names Rust std, Node.js, Chromium and signal(3); app-platform 20 passed debug and `--release`; signed. Checker 1 2026-10-28: the bytes match INT-06.14 to .20 (17 or 25 with lifetime, confirm and provider in that order; 18; one 19, never `13 09`; 11/12 with the count bound); the 2 s and 256 KiB bounds hold (a full socket queue is refused at once, probed); hostile replies give MI06-02, never a panic. Keys stay in wiping types, the add message is one exact-size wiped buffer, OpenSSH format only, DSA and old PEM refused, the rounds checked before any key derivation (T22 under 50 ms), nothing logged; no agent process left after the runs. Tests: app-sshagent 8 + 24, app-crypto and app-platform pass; clippy, fmt and `cargo deny check` clean. `pipe.rs` unsafe, Checker 1 verdict: sound, SAFETY note true, SIG_ERR and errno right. Ignoring SIGPIPE once at start is the standard for the program that owns main (Rust's runtime, Node.js, Chromium); per-socket SO_NOSIGPIPE alone is not enough: Rust already sets it on `connect`, `bind` and `accept` sockets, but not on `UnixStream::pair` or pipes (probe with SIGPIPE at default: a pair write ends the process, exit 141). AT-J03 signed with conditions (LD-182). Issues: (1) as open point (1) above, confirmed by a probe; (2) t31 is flaky, 1 failure in 10 runs (tests/agent.rs:522): the process-wide one-request lock lets t27's silent agent (2 s) or t68 (1 s) hold back t31's first list past the 2 s lifetime; at_h01's 1 s and t27's 1.9 to 2.6 s window are exposed the same way; give t27, t68, t31, t33 and at_h01 one shared test lock; (3) every run leaves /tmp/e9s1-‹pid› behind (demo keys, fake sockets; 12 found and deleted): remove per-test folders on drop as `Throwaway` does, and put the keys in `CARGO_TARGET_TMPDIR`; (4) no fuzz target for key reading (Rust rule 10): add one (`SshKey::read`, then `unlock` for unprotected keys) to the check.sh smoke. New finding: App refuses 1 in 256 valid P-256 keys that `ssh-keygen` makes. `ssh-key` 0.7.0-rc.11 `EcdsaPrivateKey::decode` sets `MIN_SIZE = 32`, so it refuses a private scalar that OpenSSH stores in 31 bytes (top byte zero). A plain key then gets MI06-10 and a protected key MI06-15, although its password is right. Probe: 400 throwaway keys gave 1 refusal; `ssh-keygen -y` reads that key. The same cause makes `tests/ssh.rs` fail in about 1 run in 17: each test makes a new K2 (seen once in 10 runs, `int06_24_lock_removes…` at ssh.rs:60). Fix on our side (OD-04): patch `ssh-key` as `vendor/zxcvbn` is patched, so a shorter scalar is padded, with a fixed demo P-256 key that has a 31-byte scalar and a test that fails with the patch undone. ## E10 Hardware keys (app-hwkey)

**E10-S1 Detect keys and slots; challenge-response** (INT-07); AT-H03 and AT-H04 pass; tested with a simulated key (OD-01), and with a real key when the owner plugs one in. Status: **Done** 2026-10-28 (Verify signed, software-only proof per OD-01; real-key checks open). Proof in [E10-S1-hardware-keys.md](../security/parity/E10-S1-hardware-keys.md). Built 2026-10-28 (crates/app-hwkey: detection of the INT-07.6 keys (vendor and product ids, slots 1 and 2 from the status bits, the serial, or "serial unknown" listed as any key), the 1-byte test challenge that never waits for a touch, the HMAC-SHA1 frame protocol (CRC low byte first, busy bit, reply count, ones' complement CRC check `F0B8`, reset report), touch wait capped at 20 s, `NotPermitted` with MI07-01 and MI07-02 as data; one `Bus` trait under both the IOKit layer `usb/` (objc2-io-kit, 5 `unsafe` blocks with SAFETY notes) and the simulated device of INT-07.30 (tests only; its slots answer through app-format's software key, HMAC in app-crypto); `HardwareKey` implements app-format's `ChallengeResponse`, so the 20-byte answer goes into the FMT-04 composite key in `SecretBytes`; one device operation at a time. `cargo test -p app-hwkey`: 32 passed (T01 to T21, T25 to T30, T32 to T35, T49, FMT-04.6-a; AT-H03 and AT-H04 against the simulated key), 1 IOKit listing smoke passed, T53 real key ignored (OD-01); T47: no IOKit symbol in the simulated test binary; T45: only IOKit and CoreFoundation linked; clippy and fmt clean; cargo deny ok. Open: the device-arrival look (INT-07.14) and the coalesced second detection (INT-07.13) wait for their callers; AT-J03 for objc2-io-kit and objc2-core-foundation). Checker 1 2026-10-28: vectors T03, T13, T14, T16, T18 and the padded challenge recomputed in python3 and equal; 33 passed, 1 ignored; clippy, fmt, cargo deny ok; 5 of 5 code breaks caught (CRC polynomial, 20 s touch cap, `NotPermitted` fallback, reply CRC check, zero-part skip), sources restored (`cmp` equal); no simulator in the release library; AT-J03 signed with conditions (LD-188). Issues: (1) LD-175 (R07-2) gives `NotPermitted` in app-format's `HardwareKeyReason` to this story: add it, map it one to one in `lib.rs` `From<Reason>`, give app-ffi's `hardware_key_message` the MI07-01 arm and its test list the new reason, so an unlock shows MI07-01 (INT-07.20, T11), not M06-20; (2) serial 0 asks the first key whose slot bit is set, not the first usable one (INT-07.5): a key listed first with a one-time password in that slot ends the challenge with `SlotNotConfigured` and a second, usable key is never asked; with serial 0, a `SlotNotConfigured` reply moves on to the next key, plus a test with such a key listed first. Unsafe, per block: `set_device_matching(None)` sound; `CFSetGetValues` sound (buffer of `count` pointers, the set an owned immutable copy); `CFRetained::retain` sound (Get rule, +1, released on drop, null checked); `IOHIDDeviceGetReport` sound (8 bytes, length checked to 8, IOReturn mapped); `IOHIDDeviceSetReport` sound (8 bytes read only, IOReturn mapped); location id low 32 bits correct; sent back (2). Fixed 2026-10-28: `NotPermitted` reaches the key flow one to one (app-format `HardwareKeyReason`, app-ffi MI07-01 line and its test lists, app-cli's text match, which also has no wildcard; T11 expects `NotPermitted`); with serial 0 a `SlotNotConfigured` moves on to the next key (`t07_serial_0_asks_the_first_usable_key`); app-format's software key is built only with its tests-only `software-key` feature (INT-07.29; only app-format's and app-hwkey's tests turn it on). Re-check: app-hwkey 33 passed, 1 passed and 1 ignored (T53); app-format all suites pass; app-ffi `messages` 7 passed; app-cli builds; clippy (app-hwkey, app-format, app-cli) and fmt clean. 4 of 4 breaks caught, each undone and `cmp` equal: `NotPermitted` mapped to `Failed` fails T11; app-ffi's arm set to M06-20 fails `t39_app_06_18_every_kind`; no move-on for serial 0 fails `t07_serial_0_asks_the_first_usable_key`; app-hwkey's tests without `software-key` do not compile (`SoftwareKey`, `SlotMode` unresolved). `cargo tree --workspace -e features,normal`: 0 `software-key` (2 with dev edges: app-format's and app-hwkey's tests); release build of app-cli and app-hwkey: the release app-format library holds no `SoftwareKey` code (only its name in the metadata), the release `app` binary none. Unsafe blocks unchanged. Checker 1 re-check 2026-10-28: passed. Unsafe second review 2026-10-28 (Rust rule 2): all 5 blocks sound, SAFETY notes true. The C types match the SDK headers (`CFIndex` is `signed long` = `isize`, `IOReturn` is `kern_return_t` = `int`, `IOOptionBits` is `UInt32`, `IOHIDReportType` Feature = 2; GetReport `uint8_t *` and `CFIndex *`, SetReport `const uint8_t *`, written through never; `CFSetGetValues` gets a buffer of `CFSetGetCount` pointers). Create and Copy references are taken once (`from_raw`) and the set's Get-rule items get one retain each, released on every exit path; a NULL from Copy gives an empty list. The IOKit types are neither `Send` nor `Sync` in the bindings, and no run loop, queue or callback is set. Devices open and close with `kIOHIDOptionsTypeNone`; no seize, no manager open and no input report anywhere in the crate. Location id `as u32` keeps the bits of an `SInt32` number that `CFNumberGetValue` sign-extends: correct. Proof: debug and `--release`: 33 passed, then 1 passed and 1 ignored (T53); IOKit listing in both builds: 2 HID devices, 0 with a key vendor id; `leaks --atExit` on the listing test: 0 leaks; clippy `-D warnings` clean; unsafe second Checker 2026-10-28: signed. Verify F1 (LD-190) built 2026-10-28: plug-in and removal events on the device interface (`Bus::watch`, `Change`) and in the simulated device (plug in, pull out); `usb/watch.rs` registers the IOKit matching and removal callbacks for vendor ids 1050 and 1D50 on a run loop of its own thread, skips the devices present at the start, never opens the manager or a device and registers no input report callback, and stops within 0.1 s, joined on drop (7 new `unsafe` blocks with SAFETY notes); `KeyWatcher` looks at start, 0.5 s after the last plug-in, and on "Look for keys again", no look on removal, requests during a look fold into one more. Tests: T22 (the challenge waits for a running detection; two requests during a look give one more look), T23, T24, and an IOKit watch smoke on this Mac without a key; `cargo test -p app-hwkey`: 37 passed, then 1 passed and 1 ignored (T53), then 1 passed (watch); each part reverted fails its test; `leaks --atExit` on the watch test: 0 leaks; clippy and fmt clean. Unsafe review of `usb/watch.rs` next. Watch check by Checker 1: 37 passed, then 1 passed and 1 ignored (T53), then 1 passed (watch); clippy and fmt clean; `leaks --atExit` on the watch test: 0 leaks (975 nodes); 0.00 s of CPU in the 0.54 s watch test, so the run loop waits and does not spin; `nm -u` on the watch test: only `IOHIDManagerCreate`, `SetDeviceMatchingMultiple`, `CopyDevices`, the two `Register...Callback`, `ScheduleWithRunLoop`, `UnscheduleFromRunLoop` and the CoreFoundation calls; no `IOHIDManagerOpen`, no input report or value call, no seize; the unit-test binary has no IOKit symbol. 4 of 4 breaks caught, each undone and `cmp` equal: the 0.5 s delay set to 0.1 s fails T23; a look on removal fails T24; detection without the process lock fails T22; the watch's drop without the join fails the IOKit watch test 3 of 3 runs. LD-188's conditions hold with the new calls (note added to its condition (3)). Notes, not blocking: after unscheduling, also clear both callbacks (register `None` with a null context) so that no IOKit path can reach the context after `Watching` drops; list `CFArray`, `CFDictionary` and `CFRunLoop` among app-hwkey's own core-foundation features and in the approved table (today `hid` turns them on, so the compiled set is unchanged); the skip of keys present at the start has no test until a real key (T53). Unsafe, per block: (1) reading `kCFRunLoopDefaultMode` sound (an immutable extern static, `None` handled); (2) `set_device_matching_multiple` sound (an array of CFString-to-CFNumber dictionaries made with the CF type callbacks, which retain; every Create reference taken once and released on drop); (3) registering both callbacks sound (the `IOHIDDeviceCallback` signature exactly, and the context is a `&Watching` used read-only; it is registered after `Watching` exists, and callbacks reach it only from this thread's run loop, which runs only inside the stop loop); (4) `schedule_with_run_loop` sound (this thread's own run loop and the default mode; the manager is confined to the thread, neither `Send` nor `Sync`); (5) `unschedule_from_run_loop` sound (the same run loop and mode, before `Watching`, the device set and the manager drop in reverse order; every exit after the schedule reaches it); (6) and (7) the context deref in `arrived` and `removed` sound (shared reads only, on the watch thread, while `Watching` lives; the sink only sends on an unbounded channel and does not panic). The stop path always joins (`UsbWatch::drop`, and a failed start drops the watch); `KeyWatcher::drop` sends a stop, drops the watch, then joins. Checker 1 (watch) 2026-10-28: passed. Unsafe second review of `usb/watch.rs` 2026-10-28 (LD-190, Rust rule 2): all 8 blocks sound, SAFETY notes true. (1) `kCFRunLoopDefaultMode` is an immutable extern static, and `None` is handled. (2) The matching array holds `VendorID` CFString keys and CFNumber values (`kIOHIDVendorIDKey`). `from_slices` and `from_retained_objects` use the CF type callbacks, which retain, and every Create reference is released once on drop. (3) The callbacks match `IOHIDDeviceCallback` in `IOHIDBase.h` (`void *` context, `IOReturn`, `void *` sender, and the `IOHIDDeviceRef` device, non-null inside `CF_ASSUME_NONNULL`); the binding's fn-pointer type checks this at compile time. The context points to `watching`, which is never moved or mutated, and is registered after `watching` exists. (4) and (5) Schedule and unschedule use this thread's run loop (`CFRunLoopGetCurrent`, Get rule, retained once) and the default mode. The manager and run loop are neither `Send` nor `Sync`, so they stay on the watch thread. (6) and (7) The context is only read as a shared `&Watching`, inside `run_in_mode` on this thread. (8) Clearing both callbacks (`None`, null context; both parameters are `_Nullable` in `IOHIDManager.h`) is needed and correct: locals drop in reverse order, so `watching` drops before `present` and `manager`, and after (8) the manager holds no pointer to it. `present` outlives every callback, so the `known` addresses cannot be reused. Stop path: `UsbWatch::drop` always sets the flag and joins; a failed start (`false` sent, or the thread ended before sending) drops the watch, which joins. Notes, not blocking: (a) block 8's note would read better as "registering `None` with a null context (both `_Nullable` in `IOHIDManager.h`) replaces the stored callback and context"; (b) the binding's callback type is `C-unwind`, so a panicking sink would unwind through CFRunLoop frames in debug builds (release aborts, Rust rule 11); today's sinks only send on a channel and cannot panic. LD-188 holds: pins `=0.3.2`; the new core-foundation names are all turned on by `hid` already; only `usb/` imports the two crates; the library imports 13 IOKit functions (`nm -u`, debug rlib), with no `IOHIDManagerOpen`, no queue, input report or input value call, and no seize anywhere in the source. Proof: debug and `--release`: 37 passed, then 1 passed and 1 ignored (T53), then 1 passed (watch); `nm -u` on the watch test, both builds: 7 `IOHIDManager` calls and the CF calls only; `leaks --atExit` on the watch test, both builds: 0 leaks (944 and 947 nodes); `/usr/bin/time` on the watch test: real 0.54 s, user 0.00 s, sys 0.00 s (no spin); clippy `-D warnings` clean; unsafe second Checker (watch) 2026-10-28: signed

## E11 Shared groups and remote sync (app-share)

Scope decided by the owner after E1 (cards INT-08, INT-09). W-15 decided 2026-10-31 (LD-262 (6)): shared groups (INT-08) are in, as the free family option with no server; remote sync by commands (INT-09) is out.
Order: E11-S1 and E11-S4 start at once. E11-S2 waits for E11-S1; if E11-S1 finds ZIP64 end or central records in reference containers, the lead rules on LD-142 (2) before E11-S2 starts (LD-267 (5)). E11-S3 waits for E11-S2; it shares app-ffi with E16-S7a and the Swift disk watch with E15-S3, so it never runs at the same time as either (team.md). E11-S5 waits for E11-S3 and E11-S4. E11-S6 waits for E11-S5. E16-S8 waits for E11-S4 (both edit design/option-a/proposals.html, LD-268). Card: [INT-08](../spec/integrations/INT-08-shared-groups.md); D-INT08-1 to D-INT08-10 take the card's recommended options (the default rule of spec/decisions.md; D-INT08-3 is LD-61), so App writes no signatures (D-INT08-1 (a)) and INT-08.29, INT-08.30 and every "option b" part are not built.

**E11-S1 INT-08 card check.** A Verify agent (reference read only; the reference app runs hidden, rule 10) checks the card's "check with Verify" markers: the XML declaration of INT-08.3, the success-message setting of INT-08.7 and its test T10, the zip64 fields of INT-08.9, and the runtime behaviour the card read from the source ("How this was learned"). A Spec agent writes the results into the card, records there that D-INT08-1 to D-INT08-10 are taken by the default rule (LD-61 for D-INT08-3), and points the card's Status and Epics lines at E11-S2 to E11-S6. It edits only the INT-08 card; the lead updates INT-08's line in spec/README.md after it (LD-267).
- Checks: each marker is confirmed or corrected with proof (the bytes of a container the reference wrote from a demo vault, or an offscreen screenshot), or carried open with its reason when it needs the reference app's window and the owner's go (rule 10); every changed rule keeps at least one test case; no INT-09 rule is built or changed (LD-262 (6)); a second Spec agent reviewed the edits; the name guard passes, and the card's encoded names stay hex.
- Status: **Done** 2026-10-31 (Verify read the reference source and ran the installed reference hidden on demo vaults; Spec writer, then the second Spec review passed in round 2): INT-08.3 confirmed; INT-08.7 and T10 corrected (the box stays unticked within one run, shows ticked after every start), T10 open (needs the reference window, W-08); INT-08.9 made exact (every local header has a 20-byte ZIP64 extra field of zeros; no ZIP64 end, locator or central-directory data, so app-core's ZIP reader reads reference zips unchanged and LD-142 (2) needs no ruling, LD-267 (5)); INT-08.13, INT-08.10 (empty signer name) and the reference's file modes (unsigned 0600, signed 0644) added; 5 reference containers kept as hex in crates/app-core/tests/data/int08/ with their SHA-256 in the card, all 5 opened by `app`; a 'Not run in E11-S1' row lists hidden-runnable reference checks for E11-S6; D-INT08-1 to D-INT08-10 taken by the default rule; name guard PASS. Lead notes: the reference halves of INT-08.37 and INT-08.40 go into E11-S6's Verify brief; the first hidden launch showed a reference window for about 25 s (LD-270).

**E11-S2 Share settings and containers.** The crate `app-share` (GPL-3.0-or-later, `publish = false`; INT-08 only): the share settings item and its XML (INT-08.1 to INT-08.4), the path (INT-08.5), the share key (INT-08.6), reading unsigned and zip containers by their first bytes within the vault limits through app-core's own ZIP reader, which app-core exposes to app-share for this (INT-08.8, INT-08.9, INT-08.36; LD-142, no zip crate; LD-267 (5)), and building and atomically writing unsigned containers (INT-08.12 to INT-08.14, INT-08.33, INT-08.35). No signatures (D-INT08-1 (a)) and no new outside crate.
- Checks: every INT-08 test case for these rules passes; unsigned containers move both ways through the reference command line tool on demo vaults (a container App wrote opens in it with the share password, and App reads one it made); App reads the zip containers the reference app made from E11-S1's bytes, or this check stays open with its reason (rule 10, the owner's go), as E15-S5 does, and it holds back none of the unsigned checks (LD-267 (6)); a zip entry stops at the vault file limit (FMT-01.31; AT-A06), a KDF cost in the ask band or above the CRY-02 limits is refused without a question (AT-A05, LD-281 (1)), and a hostile document inside a container is handled (AT-A08); fuzz targets for the settings parser and the zip container reader run in the fuzz smoke (AT-A10); the share password is a `SecretString` and appears in no `Debug` output or message (INT-08.39, SR-14); containers are written 0600 (SD-09, AT-I06); two Checkers (file format and secret handling).

**E13-S8 Zero-knowledge proof run** (LD-340). Demo accounts get known secrets: account passwords, Recovery Kits, entry text, file and folder names, passkey and SSH private keys, a shared vault, a QR link. Then every flow runs on 127.0.0.1: sign-up, a new Mac, sync, sharing and removal, the web vault, E21-S6's link. It runs first after E17-S2, and again after E18-S5, E20-S10, E21-S6 and E24-S6.
- Checks: the full server store, its logs and every request it got, read byte by byte, hold none of the secrets: not as text, base64, hex, or a SHA-256 of them; with the whole store and every server-side secret, a test cannot open any vault or tell a right password guess from a wrong one (AT-L06); the "server blind" line passes; App's own text says "zero-knowledge" and never makes "end-to-end" the main claim; findings filed in security/findings.md; no Critical or High open.
