## E15 Cloud-folder sync hardening (macos/, app-core)

Goal: a vault in iCloud Drive, Dropbox, OneDrive, Google Drive or a network folder never loses a change and never brings a deleted entry back. No server. Built now (LD-262 (2)). Design and sources: [spec/research/sync-server.md](../spec/research/sync-server.md), P2 (LD-265, LD-267 (2)), the key rule of P3 (LD-264) and P18 (with its rollback guard, LD-267 (3)).
Order across E15 to E19: E15-S1 starts at once (with E11-S1 and E11-S4). E16-S1 waits for E15-S1 (both edit MOD-11; team.md: two stories never edit one file at once). E18-S1 waits for E16-S1 (both add to spec/README.md's list, and SHR-03 builds on SYN-05). E15-S2 is a blocker for the E16 sync client (E16-S7a). E19 and the E17 billing stories wait (LD-262 (2)).
Order in E15: E15-S1 starts at once. E15-S2 waits for E15-S1 and E16-S1 (it builds the key rule E16-S1 writes, LD-264). E15-S3 waits for E15-S1; it owns other files than E15-S2 and runs in parallel with it. E15-S4 waits for E15-S1 and E15-S2 (its checks need the deletion and key rules), and for E15-S3 (both change the reload path of APP-05.26). E15-S5 waits for E15-S2 to E15-S4 and the owner's yes.

**E15-S1 Card edits for cloud folders.** A Spec agent writes the deletion rule of LD-265 into every text that says otherwise: FMT-15.10, FMT-15.11 and MOD-11.15 (every same-vault merge runs the FMT-15.11 deletion pass over the whole vault; same vault has the one meaning of LD-267 (2), which it writes into MOD-11's Notation for every same-vault rule (the deletion pass, the metadata and the key): the same root group id, and opened with this vault's key or with credentials the user gave for that copy (MOD-11.29), the key or the user's own credentials being the proof; a copy under a key this Mac retired gets the content merge only, never its key and never its deletions; share imports and the import wizard never apply deletions; a parity note says this goes beyond the reference app), the MOD-11 notes "No lost data" and SI-09, and FMT-15's security notes, each with LD-265's reason (only a key holder can make a copy that opens with the vault's key); SD-03 in plan/security.md and AT-I05 in plan/attacks-and-checks.md get LD-265's wording. It also edits APP-05.10 (the `flock` covers one Mac only), APP-05.17 (the conflict-copy finder, which never lists the backup path APP-05.20 makes for this vault or App's own temp files) and APP-05.24 to APP-05.30 (coordinated reads and writes, the "Still downloading" state shown where APP-05.30's "It may still be syncing" line is today). By LD-267 (1) it also edits APP-05.29: `MSG-DISK-NEW-KEY` and its note ask for "the password or key this copy opens with", with no "changed elsewhere"; its last sentence reads "after a merge the vault keeps the key LD-264 picks; after a discard it takes the disk copy's key"; and it adds the message that a copy uses a replaced password (LD-267 (2)). By LD-267 (3) it writes the rollback guard of P18 (6) into APP-05.26: an older disk version is never reloaded silently, but merged as a same-vault copy with a warning that the file went back in time. LD-269 (3), (4) amends this story.
- Checks: every new or changed rule has an ID and at least one test case (MOD-11 T21 gets its new expected result; T51 gets a case with an older key on disk whose result keeps this Mac's key, LD-267 (1); APP-05.26 gets a case where an older copy replaces the file, LD-267 (3)); MOD-11 holds one definition of a same-vault merge, in its Notation (LD-267 (2)); no APP-05 text still asks for "the new" credentials or says the key was "changed elsewhere"; no card, note or check text still says that a same-vault merge never applies deletions or that deletions apply only to signed shares (a grep for the old sentences finds none); SD-03 and AT-I05 read as LD-265 words them; a second Spec agent reviewed the edits; the APP-05 and MOD-11 parity rows name the change; every choice links spec/research/sync-server.md; the name guard passes.
- Status: **Done** 2026-10-31 (Verify signed in round 2; Spec writer, second Spec review 2 rounds, Verify's 4 round-1 fails fixed): MOD-11's Notation holds the one same-vault definition (LD-267 (2)); FMT-15.10, FMT-15.11, MOD-11.15, the notes "No lost data" and SI-09, FMT-15's security notes, SD-03 and AT-I05 carry LD-265's rule and reason; APP-05.10, APP-05.17, APP-05.24 to APP-05.30 rewritten (coordinated access, "Still downloading", the rollback guard of P18 (6) in APP-05.26 with an immediate save and MSG-DISK-ROLLBACK, MSG-DISK-OLD-KEY with Skip and Merge anyway, APP-05.29's words); INT-09.18 and INT-09.24 follow the retired-key exception; tests T21, T51 (b), T55, T61 to T65, T70 to T76; grep for the old sentences clean; name guard PASS. Lead: the share-item limit is narrowed by LD-271, which E16-S1 writes into MOD-11.15, FMT-15.11 and T65.

**E15-S2 Deleted entries stay deleted; the newer key in sync merges.** app-core's merge runs the FMT-15.11 deletion pass in every same-vault merge (change on disk, conflict copy, server sync; LD-265), and in the server-sync and cloud-folder merges the result takes the newer of the two keys (MOD-11.19 limit 2 as E16-S1 writes it, LD-264), except a copy under a key this Mac retired, which gets the content merge only, never its key and never its deletions (LD-267 (2)); app-core makes the retired-key record (a salted SHA-256 of the transformed key, with the KDF settings and seed it was made with, P2) at a credential change and when a merge replaces a key, and tests a copy's credentials against the records; the shared-group import keeps its own scope (D-11-9), and it and the import wizard never take a key. LD-269 (2), (3) amends this story.
- Checks: MOD-11 T21 gives the new result of E15-S1; T55 (d) of E16-S1 passes and T55 (a) to (c) keep their results; a copy whose credentials match a retired-key record adds its entries but neither its key nor its deletions, also when it was saved again with a fresh KDF seed (FMT-04.17), and the record holds no key or password bytes; SD-03 and AT-I05 pass as LD-265 words them; a root-share container (same root group id, opened with the share password) is not taken as the same vault: no key is taken and its merge stays the shared-group import; a property test of 1,000 random pairs of copies of one vault: no entry deleted on one side comes back unless it was edited or moved after the deletion (LD-271 (2)), and no newer live entry is lost; a share's item dated 2099 is stored at the import time and an entry moved out of the shared group after that time stays (MOD-11 T65, FMT-15 15-26 and 15-27); the E4-S12 merge tests still pass; Mac QA: a change-on-disk merge keeps a deletion made on the other copy; two Checkers (data loss).
- Status: Done 2026-11-01 (Verify signed; Builder; Checker 1 passed in round 4 (LD-283); Checker 2 in 3 rounds; F-152 and F-154 fixed under LD-287, F-155 under LD-288 (1); F-146 fixed): every same-vault merge runs the whole-vault deletion pass, comparing the later of Mod and Loc that either side holds, with one source-time map per kind; change-on-disk, conflict-copy and server-sync merges take the newer key; the retired-key record holds a fresh salt, SHA-256 of T and the KDF settings and seed, no key bytes, and a copy under a retired key gets the content merge only; a share's items are clamped to the import time; T21 checked against the reference command line tool (it brings the entry back, App keeps it deleted; with another root id App gives the reference result); F-146, F-152, F-154 and F-155 re-tested; 15 Verify mutants killed; 2588 Rust tests pass; the property test passed 3 more times with new seeds; the visible Mac QA walk goes to W-08 (the offscreen app-ffi test passes); LD-288 (2) and the app-ffi wiring stay with E15-S4

**E15-S3 Coordinated file access and "Still downloading".** Swift wraps the unlock read, the reload read and the pre-save checksum in a coordinated read, and the save's rename in a coordinated write (`.forReplacing`), on a background queue; a dataless file (`SF_DATALESS`) shows "Still downloading" and starts the download (P18 (1), (2)).
- Checks: a test file presenter in another process is told of App's save, and App's reload sees that process's coordinated write; a test fails if unlock, reload or save does file I/O on the main thread; a file reported dataless through a test seam shows the state and never blocks the window; EDEADLK in the 30-second check counts as "no change"; every APP-05 save test still passes; Mac QA screenshot; tests launch hidden (rule 10).
- Status: Done 2026-10-31 (Verify signed; Builder, Checker 1 in 4 rounds, LD-277, LD-280): every read of the vault file (unlock, reload, merge, the pre-save checksum) is coordinated, and the save's rename is a coordinated .forReplacing write, all off the main thread; a presenter in another process is told of App's read and replacing write, and App's reload sees its coordinated write whole (T72); a dataless file shows 'Still downloading', starts the download once and never blocks the window (T73); the 30-second tick never fetches, and EDEADLK counts as no change; F-144 re-tested and fixed; 10 Verify mutants killed; 370 Swift tests pass (APP-01.27 c passed alone after a load failure), 2531 Rust tests pass; Mac QA screenshots offscreen, light and dark; a save by App opens in the reference command line tool; F-145 goes to E15-S4 (LD-280 (2)), and LD-285 (3) adds two notes to E15-S4

**E15-S4 Conflict-copy finder.** LD-320 amends this story (F-236: a state file with one value that does not read is never written over). LD-313 amends this story (F-173: Merge anyway drops this Mac's own item for an object it brings back, MOD-11.31, T76). LD-277 (4), LD-280 (2), LD-283 (3), (4), LD-285 (3), LD-288 (2) and LD-291 amend this story (the `SF_DATALESS` guard in `header_look`; coordinate the off-main reads that still fetch). iCloud conflict versions (NSFileVersion) and sibling copies in the vault's folder (same extension, name starting with the vault's base name, opens with the current key, same root group id) are merged with MOD-11; a sibling that does not open with the current key is listed as needing its password and merges only after the user gives the credentials it opens with, the result keeping the key LD-264 picks, and a copy under a key this Mac retired getting the content merge only (MOD-11.29, LD-264, LD-267 (2)); each copy goes to the Trash only after the user says OK; the backup path of APP-05.20 for this vault and App's own temp files are never listed (P18 (3)). It also builds the new APP-05.29 words of E15-S1 and the replaced-password message (LD-267 (1), (2)) and the rollback guard of P18 (6) (LD-267 (3)); local state, never synced, holds E15-S2's retired-key records and, per vault, the newest change time seen and the SHA-256 of the last bytes read or written. LD-269 (3), (4) amends this story. LD-302 (4) amends this story: F-166, the release-mode test build of app-ffi (`cargo test --release -p app-ffi --lib`) compiles and passes.
- Checks: next to a demo vault, two same-vault copies named in the Dropbox and OneDrive patterns are found and merged, and a file with another name stem is left alone; with backups on, App's own `.old` copy next to the vault is never listed, and neither is a leftover App temp file; a copy with the same name but another root group id is not merged; a copy that does not open with the current key is listed as needing its password and left alone until the user gives the credentials it opens with; LD-267 (2)'s case in one cloud folder: Mac A changes the password while Mac B saves under the old key; both edits survive, the result has A's key, and A says the copy uses a replaced password; B asks for the new password; B's deletion made under the old key is deleted everywhere after B's next sync; a planted copy under A's old password with a key-change time in the future neither brings the old key back nor deletes anything on A; T51's older-key case keeps this Mac's key (LD-267 (1)); an older copy put in place of the vault gives the went-back-in-time warning, and no newer entry or KDF setting is lost (P18 (6), LD-267 (3)); nothing moves to the Trash without OK; the iCloud path is tested through a seam over NSFileVersion (real iCloud in E15-S5); a merged copy brings back no deleted entry (E15-S2). LD-288 (2): when this Mac retires a key with a password plus another part (a key file or a hardware key), its record also holds a second salted digest, of the transformed key made from the password alone with the record's salt, KDF settings and seed; is_retired matches either digest (MOD-11.31, T75). Making that record runs the KDF twice (once for a version 3 key of a password and a hardware key only); a record of a key with one part holds one digest. T75: the record of `demo` plus key file K matches `demo` alone and `demo` plus K, never K alone, `demo` plus another key file K2, or `demo-x` plus K; a planted copy under `demo` alone shows MSG-DISK-OLD-KEY before any merge (MSG-SYNC-OLD-KEY in server sync), Skip the default, and Merge anyway takes neither its key, its MasterKeyChanged nor its deletions. No digest equals K_enc or K_mac of any file, and no record holds key or password bytes. T74: a record of a version 3 file keyed by a password plus a hardware key also matches the password alone (FMT-04.15). LD-285 (3): closing a vault whose unlock waits for its file stops that attempt and wipes its typed password; the ExportScreensTests leave no TMPDIR folder, also when they fail. F-145 (LD-280 (2)) gets its test.
- Status: Done 2026-11-01 (Verify signed; Builder, Checker 1 in 4 rounds, Checker 2 in 4 rounds; LD-313, LD-315, LD-319, LD-320, LD-324): sibling copies in Dropbox and OneDrive names are merged, and another name stem, another root id, the .old backup and temp files are left alone; a copy under another password waits for its credentials; T69's two Macs converge under A's key; a planted copy under the old password with a 2099 key time takes nothing; T51 (b) keeps this Mac's key; the rollback guard merges an older file and loses no newer entry or KDF setting; nothing goes to the Trash without OK; iCloud is tested through the NSFileVersion seam; T74 to T77 pass; F-145, F-166, F-173, F-216, F-224, F-225 and F-236 re-tested; copies the reference command line tool made merge in App with their deletion kept, and App's save opens in that tool; 2934 Rust tests and 390 Swift tests pass (hidden); F-240's test added by the lead (LD-324 (2)); F-226, F-227 and F-241 go to E16-S7a. Earlier: started 2026-11-01

## E16 Sync server and client (app-proto, app-sync, app-server, app-crypto, app-platform, macos/)

Goal: two Macs (two in-process test clients) sync one vault through App's own server on 127.0.0.1 with no change lost, and the server cannot read it. Built now on this Mac only (LD-262 (2)); the outside audit (D6) comes before any server ships. Design and sources: [spec/research/sync-server.md](../spec/research/sync-server.md) P1 to P7, P14 to P17, P20 to P22, P25 (the lead's rulings LD-263 to LD-267); parts: [architecture.md](architecture.md), "Sync server and sharing (E15 to E19)".
Order: E16-S1 waits for E15-S1 (both edit MOD-11). E16-S2, E16-S3 and E16-S6 wait for E16-S1 and for the lead's AT-J03 yes on their crates; each changes `Cargo.lock` and `vendor/`, so they run one at a time: E16-S6 after E16-S2 (its check uses E16-S2's P-256 verify, LD-267 (4)), and E16-S3 in any slot. E16-S4 waits for E16-S3. E16-S5 waits for E16-S2 and E16-S4. E16-S7a waits for E16-S5, E16-S6 and E15-S2. E16-S7b waits for E16-S7a (LD-266 (6)). E16-S8 waits for E16-S1, E18-S1 and E11-S4 (both edit design/option-a/proposals.html, LD-268). E16-S9 waits for E16-S7b and E16-S8. Most stories of E16 to E18 edit app-proto, app-sync or app-server: where this order lets two of them start together and they share a file, the lead runs them one at a time (team.md).

**E16-S1 Spec cards for sync and accounts.** A Spec agent writes, in the card template of spec/README.md and added to its list: `spec/sync/` SYN-01 Wire protocol (P3), SYN-02 Server store (P15), SYN-03 Client sync and the rollback guard (P3, P4, P7; with the key change of LD-264 and the one meaning of "same vault" that E15-S1 writes into MOD-11's Notation (LD-267 (2)): the result takes the newer of the two keys, except that a copy under a key this Mac retired never gives its key or its deletions, and App says the copy uses a replaced password; when the server copy does not open with this Mac's key, App asks for the credentials that copy opens with (MOD-11.29, APP-05.29), never "the new" ones, and uploads nothing until it opens; the key-file part stays in the key), SYN-04 Devices, sign-in and sessions (P5), SYN-05 The code two screens compare (P10), SYN-06 Accounts, the account secret, the Recovery Kit and a new Mac (P6); `spec/crypto/` CRY-04 Signatures, HPKE and key wrap (P5, P6, P8). By LD-264 it edits MOD-11.19 limit 2, the AT-A09 note and T55 (a new case (d)): in server sync and in the cloud-folder merge the result takes the newer key, except from a copy under a key this Mac retired (LD-267 (2)); a share import and the import wizard never do. It uses MOD-11's one same-vault definition and writes no second one. It proposes rows for plan/security.md (what the server sees, rollback, nothing guessable on the server) and plan/attacks-and-checks.md (hostile-server attacks) for the lead.
- Checks: every rule has an ID and at least one test case; MOD-11.19 limit 2, the AT-A09 note and T55 (d) say what LD-264 and LD-267 (2) say, MOD-11 still holds one same-vault definition, T55 (a) to (c) keep their results, and the MOD-11 parity row names the change; a second Spec agent reviewed each card and edit; every choice links spec/research/sync-server.md or a standard; "How this was learned" names public sources only (the reference app has no server); the name guard passes.
- Status: **Done** 2026-10-31 (Verify signed, round 4). Seven new cards, `spec/sync/` SYN-01 to SYN-06 and `spec/crypto/` CRY-04: 85 rules and 129 tests, all in spec/README.md's list, now `Status: reviewed`. LD-264, LD-267 (2), LD-269 (3) and LD-271 (1) to (3) are in MOD-11, FMT-15, both SI-09 notes, plan/security.md SD-03 and SI-09, T55 (d) and T65; LD-273 (1) to (9) is in the cards. Six Spec reviews (the last a narrow close-out of the lead's applied texts, 0 findings). Lead wrote the SV section, the AT-L group, the FMT-18.21 note and LD-273. Name guard PASS. Next: E16-S2, E16-S3 and E16-S6, one at a time, each after the lead's AT-J03 yes on its crates.

**E16-S2 Crypto for sync and sharing.** app-crypto gets, from CRY-04: Ed25519 sign and verify, ECDSA P-256 verify, HPKE base mode seal and open for the one suite of P8, the AES-256-GCM key wrap and the two-secret key (P6); `hpke`, `x25519-dalek` and `zeroize_derive` are vendored as App's own copies (LD-221) after AT-J03. ## E17 Accounts and billing (app-sync, app-server, macos/)

Goal now (LD-262 (2)): an account that never gives the server a password, with a Recovery Kit, and new Macs approved by a code both screens show. Later (LD-262 (2)): plans, payments and license files, once E16 to E18 work. Design and sources: [spec/research/sync-server.md](../spec/research/sync-server.md) P5, P6, P10.
Order: E17-S1 waits for E16-S2, E16-S5 and E16-S7b. E17-S2 waits for E17-S1. E17-S3 waits for E16-S7b and E17-S1 (LD-266 (1)). E17-S2, E17-S3 and E18-S2 all wait for E17-S1 and all edit app-sync and app-server's store, so they run one at a time, in that order: E17-S2, then E17-S3, then E18-S2 (team.md, LD-267). E17-S4 waits for E17-S1 to E17-S3 and E16-S8. E17-S5 to E17-S7 start only when E16 to E18 work and the owner says go.

**E17-S1 Sign-up, account keys and the Recovery Kit.** Now (LD-262). The account secret, the identity and X25519 keys, the two-secret wrap and its blob on the server, the recovery public key (P6); the key-file parts of personal server vaults are now wrapped to the account's own X25519 key (P7); PUT signing of personal vaults starts here: each upload carries the identity key's Ed25519 signature over vault id, version and SHA-256 (P4, P11); the wrap through the server replaces the E16-S7b test helper's copy of the key-file part (LD-266 (2)) (SYN-06).
- Checks: given every row and file of the server and the right account password but not the account secret, a test opens nothing; the Recovery Kit and the password restore the account into an empty demo app state; a wrong password or kit gives a plain error with no detail; the account secret is `SecretBytes` in the E16-S6 device store (the this-device-only check is carried open until W-02, as in E16-S6); a key-file part reaches a second client through the server, and the test helper no longer copies it; an upload with a missing or wrong signature is refused by the other client; two Checkers.

**E17-S2 A new Mac: approval by code, or the Recovery Kit.** LD-327 (7) amends this story (recovery nonces kept apart from device nonces; `user_version` raised). Now (LD-262). The SYN-05 code compare (its first use), the HPKE seal of the account secret to the new Mac, the signed device key, 15 minutes to expire (Bitwarden's login with device); or the recovery sign-in key plus the account password. It replaces E16-S7a's test helper for a second device with the code compare.
- Checks: the E16-S7a and E16-S7b tests add their second device through the code compare, and the test helper is gone; both test clients show the same 6-digit code; a test relay that swaps a key gives different codes and nothing is sealed; a second commitment for the same request is refused (one guess); a request older than 15 minutes is refused; no device is registered before "Codes match"; the kit path registers a device; two Checkers.

The device list (name, added, last seen), revoke one device, and sign out everywhere: every session and every other device's registration go; the other Macs drop their cached account data at their next contact; local vaults are not touched.
- Checks: a revoked device gets 401 on its next call; after sign out everywhere only this Mac is registered; the other client drops its cached account data and its local vault files stay byte-identical; every session row of the account is gone from the database.

## E18 Family and team sharing (app-sync, app-proto, app-server, app-server-teams, macos/)

Goal: a family of up to 6 people and a small team share vaults through App's server on 127.0.0.1; every member is checked with a code both people compare; a removed member gets no new key, and App lists the passwords they saw. Built now on this Mac only (LD-262 (2)). Design and sources: [spec/research/sync-server.md](../spec/research/sync-server.md) P8 to P13, P19, P23 to P25 (the lead's rulings LD-263, LD-266 and LD-267).
Order: E18-S1 waits for E16-S1 (both add to spec/README.md's list, and SHR-03 builds on SYN-05). E18-S2 waits for E18-S1, E16-S5, E16-S7b and E17-S1 (LD-266 (1)), and runs after E17-S2 and E17-S3, one at a time in that order (LD-267). E18-S3 waits for E18-S2, E16-S2 and E16-S7b. E18-S4 waits for E18-S3 and E17-S2. E18-S5 waits for E18-S4. E18-S6 waits for E18-S5 (E14-S2's licence text is done). E18-S7 waits for E18-S3 to E18-S5 and E16-S8. E18-S8 waits for E18-S5.

**E18-S1 Spec cards for sharing.** A Spec agent writes, in the card template and added to spec/README.md's list: `spec/sharing/` SHR-01 Shared vault keys, wraps and epochs (P8), SHR-02 The signed member list (P9), SHR-03 Invites, roles and write checks (P10, P11; the compare itself is SYN-05), SHR-04 Removal, leaving and the list of passwords a removed member saw (P12, P13; entries deleted for good show as one line with their count and dates, for example "3 items deleted for good since <name> joined; App can no longer name them" (LD-290 (5)), because FMT-15.1 keeps only an id and a deletion time). It proposes the hostile-server attack rows (BW01, BW02, BW08, BW09, LP07, DL02, rollback, fork) for plan/attacks-and-checks.md. LD-269 (5) amends this story.
- Checks: every rule has an ID and at least one test case; a second Spec agent reviewed each card; every choice links spec/research/sync-server.md or a standard; the name guard passes.
- Status: Done 2026-11-01 (Verify signed, round 3): `spec/sharing/` SHR-01 to SHR-04, 59 rules and 102 test cases, in spec/README.md's list; three Spec reviews and Verify rounds 1 to 3; every rule has an ID and a test case naming it; every choice links spec/research/sync-server.md or a published standard or paper; LD-269 (5) is in SHR-01.2; D-SHR01-1, D-SHR01-2, D-SHR02-1, D-SHR02-2 and D-SHR04-1 decided by LD-290; name guard PASS; the plan rows (AT-L13 to AT-L22, SV-04 to SV-07, the LD-290 (3) texts) are written by the apply run of LD-292

**E18-S2 The signed member list.** app-proto, app-sync and app-server: the hash chain, the Ed25519 signatures, the rules to accept an entry, pinning, refusal of rollback and fork; the server stores the list, checks it and takes its roles from it (SHR-02).
- Checks: an entry signed by a non-admin, a gap in the sequence, a changed previous hash or an older list is refused by both client and server; a test server that holds every database row but no member key cannot add a member that clients accept (BW08, BW09); a client that saw another head refuses a forked list; a property test over random valid and invalid lists; a fuzz target for the entry parser; two Checkers.
- Status: todo

**E18-S3 Family groups and shared vaults.** A family group (Organizer, at most 2, and Member; at most 6 people) and its shared vault keyed only by a random 32-byte key; HPKE wraps signed by the admin; sync through the E16-S7a client; writes refused by the server (403) and checked by every client against the uploader's signature and role (SHR-01, SHR-03). LD-269 (5) amends this story.
- Checks: two members edit at once and both changes survive; a 7th member is refused; an upload signed by a key not in the list is refused by every client even when a test server accepts it; a wrap whose info names another vault, epoch or member does not open; the shared vault opens in app-core with its key-file part alone (FMT-04.5); a new shared vault has the lowest KDF cost App allows (CRY-02, P23, LD-267 (7)); two Checkers; a shared vault's unlock sheet asks for the account password, checked on the Mac (SHR-01.8, SHR-01 T17); quick unlock works after it (LD-290 (1)).

**E18-S4 Invites and the code compare.** The invite code (128 random bits, 5 days, one use, no key inside), the SYN-05 compare, then the signed add entry and the wrap after "Codes match"; pinned identity keys and the changed-key warning (SHR-03).
- Checks: E17-S2's compare tests pass for invites; a used or expired invite code is refused; a test server that swaps the invitee's or the admin's key at any step gives different codes, and no key is wrapped (BW01, LP07, DL02); a changed pinned key shows the warning and blocks the group until that account is removed, and the person then joins again with a new compare (SHR-02.11); a test reads the invite and finds no key.

**E18-S5 Removal and leaving.** The signed remove entry, a new key epoch, the credential change and save, new wraps for the others, the server's clean-up, and "Passwords <name> could see" with a tick per entry, plus one line with the count and dates of items deleted for good since the person joined; leaving a group; no admin reset and no key escrow (P12, P13, SHR-04).
- Checks: the removed client cannot open any version saved after its removal, and the server answers it 403; on a demo vault with known content, the list shows every entry in the vault at removal (with history, attachments and the recycle bin), and nothing else, plus one line whose count and dates match the items deleted for good after the person joined (SHR-04.7); ticks survive a restart; the words say the removed person keeps what they saw; a remove entry from a non-admin is refused; two Checkers; a person removed or who left and is then invited again keeps the keys of the new wraps on every Mac of the account (SHR-04 T21).

**E18-S6 Team features in the paid folder.** `crates/app-server-teams/` behind the cargo feature `teams`: team groups with Owner, Admin, Member and Read-only, per-vault Can view, Can edit and Can manage, many shared vaults per group (P11, P19); the folder holds a copy of `legal/TEAMS-LICENSE.md` (E14-S2) as its licence file (LD-266 (7)). The client side is GPL code in app-sync.

## E20 Web vault (app-web, app-server, app-proto, app-format, web/), now (LD-272)

Goal: a browser with no App installed opens the user's server vaults with a passkey and the account password; the vault crypto runs as App's own Rust code compiled to WebAssembly, and the server still cannot read a vault. Now (LD-272 (2)), built and tested on this Mac only (loopback with E7-S7b's test names, demo data). Hosting, a domain (it fixes the passkeys' RP ID, so it cannot change later) and a certificate browsers trust are owner steps (rule 7); the outside audit (D6) comes before it ships with the server. Design and sources: [spec/research/browser-and-web.md](../spec/research/browser-and-web.md) B13 to B30 (the lead's LD rows); parts: [architecture.md](architecture.md), "Browser extension and web vault (E7, E20)".

E20-S3 waits for E20-S2, E16-S2 and the E0-S7 follow-up (it edits vendor/, `.cargo/config.toml`, the Cargo files and scripts/), and for a yes on the `wasm32-unknown-unknown` standard library download (rule 7). E20-S4 and E20-S6 wait for E20-S1. E20-S5 waits for E20-S4. E20-S7 waits for E20-S3, E20-S5, E17-S2 and E17-S3. E20-S8a waits for E20-S6 and E20-S7; E20-S8b waits for E20-S8a. E20-S9 waits for E20-S8b. E20-S10 waits for E20-S8b and E15-S2 (the merge it runs). E20-S11 waits for E20-S8b and runs again after E20-S10. E20-S12 is later and waits for E20-S4 and E7-S7n. E20-S4, E20-S5 and E20-S8a also need E7-S7b (the driver, the test names and the certificate).
Shared files: E20-S4, E20-S5, E20-S7 and E20-S9 edit app-server (its route match and store) and app-proto, as E17-S2, E17-S3 and E18-S2 do, so they run one at a time with those (team.md, LD-267); `spec/README.md` (E20-S1 with E7-S7a, E18-S1); `design/option-a/proposals.html` (E20-S6 with E7-S7k, E11-S4, E16-S8); app-ffi (E20-S2 with E11-S3 and E16-S7a); `scripts/repro-build.sh` (E20-S4 with E7-S6 and E19-S1); `crates/app-web/` (E20-S3, E20-S7, E20-S10: in that order).

## E21 Show as QR code (app-ffi, app-cli, macos/, extension/, web/), every version (LD-336, LD-337)

Goal: every password and every protected field has one obvious action that shows it as a QR code in a pop-up, so a phone or another person can scan it; Save Image and Share for when the owner wants them, behind a clear warning. Every version of App has it: the Mac app, the `app` command line tool, the browser extension, the web vault, and any later version from its first release.
Order: E21-S1 can start at once (spec and frames only). E21-S2 waits for E21-S1. E21-S3 waits for E21-S2. E21-S4 waits for E21-S2 and E7-S7f. E21-S5 waits for E21-S2 and E20-S8b. E21-S6 is later: it waits for E17-S1, E20-S4 and hosting (an owner step, rule 7).

**E21-S1 Spec card and frames for Show as QR Code.** A Spec agent writes a new card in `spec/features/` (the next free FEA number), added to spec/README.md's list: the action and where it is (entry list, entry view, every protected field, the context menu, a shortcut, the extension's popup, the web vault, `app show --qr`); what the code holds (the value as text; the standard Wi-Fi join code for a Wi-Fi entry; FEA-04.23's code unchanged); the pop-up, its animation and Reduce Motion; the size, contrast and error-correction level that scan well from a screen; the time after which it hides, and hiding on lock (APP-02) and when the window is hidden; Save Image and Share with their warning; no image file is made unless the user saves one; nothing goes to the clipboard; whether the pop-up is kept out of screen captures, with sources. Option A frames for the pop-up and the warning, light and dark, in `design/option-a/proposals.html`.
- Checks: every rule has an ID and at least one test case; a second Spec agent reviewed the card and the frames; sources are public (ISO/IEC 18004 for QR codes, the Wi-Fi join code format, Apple's Human Interface Guidelines); the name guard passes.

- Checks: for 1,000 random values (letters, digits, symbols, spaces, emoji, other scripts, the longest password the generator makes), the drawn code, read back by Core Image's QR reader, gives the value byte for byte; a Wi-Fi entry's join code reads back with its fields; the pop-up hides on its timer, on lock and when the window is hidden; no image file exists after the pop-up unless Save Image was used; the warning shows before every Save Image and Share; nothing is written to the general pasteboard; Mac QA matches the frames, light and dark; tests launch hidden (rule 10).

## E22 Encrypt any file (app-crypto, app-files, app-cli, macos/), now (LD-336, LD-337)

Goal: the owner encrypts any file or image on demand, puts it anywhere (a USB stick, a cloud folder, an email), and opens it later with App or with any other tool that reads the same public format (age v1, LD-337 (3)). The default passphrase is made by App's generator and saved in the vault.
Order: E22-S1 can start at once (research and spec only). E22-S2 waits for E22-S1 and the owner's yes on its downloads (rule 7). E22-S3 waits for E22-S2. E22-S4 and E22-S5 wait for E22-S3. E22-S6 waits for E22-S4 and E22-S5.

**E22-S1 Research and spec cards for file encryption.** A Spec agent with web reads studies, with sources: age v1 (the C2SP specification, its test vectors, its known limits and reviews), OpenPGP (RFC 9580, with AEAD and Argon2), and the other public formats it finds; how each one fails (weak passphrases, a changed header, cut or swapped chunks, a huge work factor that stalls the opener, quantum computers and the public key parts, leaks through file names, sizes and temporary files). It writes `spec/research/file-encryption.md` and a new folder `spec/files/`, added to spec/README.md's list: FIL-01 the format and keys (passphrase, the public key mode and whether a post-quantum mode is a published standard yet), FIL-02 the screens and flows (drag a file onto App, File ▸ Encrypt File… and Decrypt File…, where the output goes, saving the passphrase as an entry, opening a file another tool made), FIL-03 limits and errors in the user's words. Option A frames for the flows, light and dark. It proposes attack rows for plan/attacks-and-checks.md and the crates to add, each with its AT-J03 reasons, for the lead.
- Checks: every rule has an ID and at least one test case; a second Spec agent reviewed each card; every choice links a public source; the cards say plainly what no format can promise (LD-337 (3)); the name guard passes.
- Status: todo

**E22-S2 The crates for file encryption.** After AT-J03 and the owner's yes on the download: the crates E22-S1 lists (for age v1: `scrypt` and ChaCha20-Poly1305), vendored as LD-221 says, with their rows in plan/architecture.md.
- Checks: AT-J03 passes for each; `cargo deny check` passes; gate G1 passes offline; check-vendor passes.
- Status: todo (the download waits for the owner's yes)

**E22-S3 File encryption in Rust.** A new crate `app-files` on app-crypto: encrypt and decrypt as a stream, so a file of any size needs little memory; the passphrase mode and the public key mode of FIL-01.
- Checks: every published test vector passes; files made by the age reference tool open, and App's files open in it (demo files, the tool from the owner's yes); one changed, cut or swapped byte anywhere gives the one generic error and no plaintext; the plaintext is never written to a temporary file; keys and passphrases are wiped after use; a work factor above FIL-03's limit is refused at once; two Checkers (crypto).
- Status: todo

**E22-S4 Encrypt File and Decrypt File in the Mac app.** FIL-02's flows: drag and drop, the File menu, a progress bar for large files, the passphrase saved as an entry.
- Checks: FIL-02's test cases; a 4 GB demo file round-trips byte for byte; cancel leaves no partial output; Mac QA matches the frames, light and dark; tests launch hidden (rule 10).

**E22-S5 `app encrypt` and `app decrypt`.** The same format in the command line tool.
- Checks: a file made by the Mac app opens with `app decrypt` and the other way round; a passphrase is never taken from the command line itself (only from a prompt, a file or the vault).

**E22-S6 Red team run against file encryption.** The Red team attacks the format, the code and the flows: changed headers, cut and swapped chunks, a huge work factor, a wrong key, a file name that tries to write outside the chosen folder, leftovers in temporary folders or memory.
- Checks: every attack is refused or handled as FIL-03 says, each by a test named after it that fails when its defence is undone (in a private copy, rule 11); no Critical or High open.

## E23 Encrypted folders (app-folders, app-ffi, macos/), now (LD-336, LD-337)

Goal: a folder anywhere (iCloud Drive, Dropbox, a USB stick) becomes an encrypted vault of files: each file and each name is encrypted on its own, so a cloud app uploads only what changed; it is fast on large files; with the format of LD-337 (4), the same folder also opens in Cryptomator on other systems.
Order: E23-S1 waits for E22-S1 (they share their research). E23-S2 waits for E23-S1 and the owner's yes on its downloads (rule 7). E23-S3 waits for E23-S2 and E22-S3. E23-S4 waits for E23-S3. E23-S5 waits for E23-S4, D7 and W-02 (a signed build). E23-S6 waits for E23-S4.

**E23-S1 Research and spec cards for encrypted folders.** A Spec agent with web reads studies, with sources: Cryptomator's published vault formats (and its newer universal format, if published), its security reviews, and other public designs it finds; what an observer of the cloud folder still learns (file count, sizes, times, folder shape); two Macs changing one file at once; how macOS can show the files (App's own window first; Apple's File Provider and FSKit later; no kernel extension); speed targets measured on this Mac. It writes `spec/research/encrypted-folders.md` and cards in `spec/files/` (FIL-04 the folder format, FIL-05 the screens, FIL-06 limits, errors and sync conflicts), with frames, attack rows and the crates to add (for Cryptomator's format: AES-SIV and AES key wrap) for the lead.

**E23-S2 The crates for encrypted folders.** After AT-J03 and the owner's yes on the download, as E22-S2.

## E24 Passkeys and SSH keys everywhere (app-crypto, app-sshagent, app-ffi, app-cli, macos/, extension/, web/), now (LD-338, LD-339)

Goal: passkeys and SSH keys work in every version of App: App is the Mac's passkey provider, passkeys move in and out by the open exchange format, SSH keys are made in App, every SSH use is approved, git commits are signed with an SSH key, and the extension, the web vault and the `app` tool each show what they can.
Order: E24-S1 can start at once (spec and frames only). E24-S2 waits for E24-S1. E24-S3 waits for E24-S2. E24-S4 waits for E24-S1, E7-S5, D7 and W-02 (a signed build). E24-S5 waits for E24-S1 and E7-S5. E24-S6 waits for E24-S2, E7-S5, E7-S7i and E20-S8b. E24-S7 waits for E24-S3 and E24-S6.

**E24-S1 Research and spec cards for passkeys and SSH keys everywhere.** A Spec agent with web reads writes the new rules into INT-04 (passkeys) and INT-06 (SSH), or new cards where they fit better, with sources: App as the Mac's passkey provider (Apple's credential provider extension, the macOS versions that allow it, what it needs from signing); passkey import and export with the FIDO Alliance's Credential Exchange Format and the macOS versions that offer it; making SSH keys (types, sizes, comments, the OpenSSH file form); App's agent for every key with an approval for each new program, and what the approval remembers; git commit signing in OpenSSH's signature format; the extension's fill of a public key; what the web vault and the `app` tool show. Option A frames for each new screen, light and dark. Attack rows for plan/attacks-and-checks.md and any crate to add, for the lead.
- Checks: every rule has an ID and at least one test case; a second Spec agent reviewed each card and edit; every choice links a public source (LD-339 (5)); the name guard passes.
- Status: todo

**E24-S2 New SSH keys made in App.** "New SSH Key" in the editor's SSH page: Ed25519 by default, and the other types E24-S1 allows; the private key stays in the vault; Copy Public Key and Save Public Key.
- Checks: each made key, saved by a test to a throwaway folder, is read by `ssh-keygen -y` and `ssh-keygen -lf` (throwaway HOME, never ~/.ssh) with the same public key and fingerprint App shows; the key signs in to a loopback `sshd` through E9's agent path; no private key file is written; Mac QA matches the frames; tests launch hidden (rule 10).
- Status: todo

**E24-S3 One agent for every key, and git signing.** App's own agent serves every key, with the approval of E24-S1 for each new program; a small `app` signing program makes OpenSSH signatures for git.
- Checks: a program asking for the first time gets App's question, and Deny signs nothing; an approved program is not asked again until the rule E24-S1 sets; in a throwaway repo and HOME, a commit signed through App passes `git verify-commit` and `ssh-keygen -Y verify`; a locked vault signs nothing; two Checkers (keys and secret handling).
- Status: todo

**E24-S4 App as the Mac's passkey provider.** Safari and other apps offer App's passkeys through Apple's credential provider extension.
- Checks: E24-S1's test cases on a signed build; a passkey made in Safari is stored in App and signs in again; a site asking for another site's passkey gets none; a locked vault offers none until unlocked.
- Status: todo (waits for a signed build: D7 and W-02)

**E24-S5 Passkeys in and out by the exchange format.** Import passkeys from another provider, and export App's, with the FIDO Alliance's Credential Exchange Format, on the macOS versions that offer it.
- Checks: E24-S1's test cases with demo passkeys; an imported passkey signs in on E7-S7b's demo pages; an export holds no key in readable form outside the format's own protection; a broken or hostile import file is refused with nothing saved.
- Status: todo

**E24-S6 Passkeys and SSH keys in the extension, the web vault and the `app` tool.** Each shows and manages what E24-S1 gives it: passkeys listed with their sites and deleted; SSH public keys shown, copied and filled into a site's "add SSH key" form.
- Checks: E24-S1's test cases in Chrome and Safari test builds, in the web vault and in `app`; no private key leaves the vault in readable form; the web vault's CSP still holds.

**E24-S7 Red team run against passkeys and SSH keys.** The Red team tries: a page or app that asks for another site's passkey; a hostile import file; a program that asks App's agent to sign without approval or by pretending to be an approved one; a forwarded agent used from another machine; a git signature made with the wrong key.
- Checks: every attack is refused or shown to the user, each by a test named after it that fails when its defence is undone (in a private copy, rule 11); no Critical or High open.
